L705: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L706: - The primary evidence to support the clinical efficacy and safety of toripalimab in patients with recurrent/metastatic NPC is from JUPITER-02. JUPITER-02 was an adequate and well-controlled, multinational, double-blind, randomized placebo-controlled trial in which 289 patients with NPC who had received no prior therapy for recurrent/metastatic disease were randomized to toripalimab plus cisplatin/gemcitabine or placebo plus cisplatin/gemcitabine. The primary endpoint is IRC-determined PFS. Key secondary endpoints are ORR and OS. This trial is ongoing.
- This is supported by data from 12 patients in Cohort 7 of POLARIS-02. These patients had not received systemic therapy for recurrent/metastatic NPC and were treated with toripalimab plus cisplatin/gemcitabine. This trial is completed.
- The primary evidence to support the clinical efficacy of toripalimab in patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC following platinum-based therapy is from 172 patients in Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02 who received toripalimab monotherapy. The key efficacy endpoints were IRC-determined ORR and DOR. This trial is completed.
- Three other trials in the safety dataset included a total of 9 patients with NPC. Due to the small number of patients in these trials, these 9 patients will not be included in the efficacy analyses.
- The primary evidence to support the safety of toripalimab is derived from (Table 74):1066 patients with a variety of tumor types across 13 trials who have received toripalimab monotherapy, which includes data from:TAB001-01 which administered toripalimab monotherapy to 139 patients in the US, including 121 patients who received toripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W.
- 190 patients with NPC in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 who received toripalimab monotherapy
- 206 patients (including 158 with NPC) who received toripalimab plus chemotherapy146 patients with NPC on JUPITER-02 who received toripalimab plus cisplatin/gemcitabine
- 60 patients (including 12 with NPC) who received toripalimab in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy on Cohorts 5 to 8 of POLARIS-02.
L707: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L708: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with the exception that the primary evidence to support the safety of toripalimab is derived from:
L709: - 851 patients with various tumor types who received toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W as a single agent, including 190 patients with NPC in Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02
- 146 patients who received toripalimab 240 mg/kg IV Q3W in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine in JUPITER-02
L710: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L711: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L712: ## 8.1.1. JUPITER-02
L713: ## Trial Design
L714: ## The Applicant's Description:
L715: JUPITER-02 is a multinational, randomized, double blinded, placebo-controlled study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine (GC) chemotherapy as first-line treatment of patients with (b) (4) locally advanced, recurrent or primary metastatic NPC.
L716: ## Eligibility Criteria Specific to JUPITER-02
L717: - Patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC
- No prior systemic therapy for recurrent or metastatic disease.
- Patients with recurrent NPC after treatment with curative intent were eligible if there was an interval of at least 6 months between recurrence and the last dose of radiotherapy or chemotherapy.
- Patients were not amenable to potentially curative locoregional treatment
- Patients with necrotic lesions at risk for massive hemorrhage (per Investigator) were not eligible.
- An archival tumor specimen or fresh biopsy tumor sample was available
- Patients taking topical ocular, intra-articular, intranasal, and inhaled corticosteroids or mineralocorticoids for orthostatic hypotension or adrenal insufficiency were eligible. Patients who received acute, low-dose, systemic immunosuppressant medications (e.g., a one-time dose of dexamethasone for nausea) are eligible. Patients with an allergic reaction to IV contrast dye requiring steroid pre-treatment must have baseline and subsequent tumor assessments performed by MRI.
- Patients with stable hypothyroidism or Type I diabetes were eligible for study entry
- No use of immunostimulatory agents within 4 weeks or 5 half-lives prior to entry
- No systemic immunostimulatory agents (e.g., interferon, IL-2) within 4 weeks/five half-lives, major surgical procedures &lt; 28 days, or use of antineoplastic traditional herbal medicine within 4 weeks prior to randomization. The need for palliative radiotherapy should be anticipated and administered prior to study entry.
- Significant cardiovascular disease, history of/active pneumonitis/pulmonary fibrosis, significant neurological or psychiatric disease, or other underlying medical conditions which in
L718: the Investigator's opinion could affect the safety of administration of study drugs and/or protocol compliance.
L719: - No \ge Grade 2 peripheral neuropathy
- No HBC antibody with detectable HCV RNA
- No known hypersensitivity to other monoclonal antibodies or to gemcitabine, cisplatin, or any of their excipients.
L720: ## Eligibility Criteria Common to POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02
L721: - Age 18-75 years
- Measurable disease per RECIST v1.1
- ECOG Performance status 0-1
- No active autoimmune disease or a history of autoimmune disease (except vitiligo, childhood asthma)
- No concomitant diseases requiring treatment with immunosuppressive drugs or corticosteroids at a dose equivalent to &gt; 10 mg/d prednisone
- No positive test for HIV, HBsAg with detectable HBV DNA
- No prior treatment with an anti-PD-1/L1, an anti-PD-L2, or an anti-CTLA4 antibody
- Toxicity from previous anticancer therapy must have recovered to grade 0-1, except alopecia, pigmentation
- No systemic anticancer therapy (except tyrosine kinase inhibitors) within 4 weeks of study entry; no tyrosine kinase inhibitors within 2 weeks of study entry; no radiation therapy (except palliative radiation to the bone) within 4 weeks of study entry
- No active tuberculosis or use of anti-tuberculosis therapy within 1 year prior to entry
- No live vaccines within 4 weeks of entry
- Patients who have received prior treatment for brain or meningeal metastases, have been stable for at least 2 months, and receiving \le 10 mg/d of prednisone are eligible for study entry
- No previous or concurrent malignant tumors other than non-melanoma skin cancer, breast carcinoma in situ, cervical carcinoma in situ, or tumors that were controlled without treatment within the previous 5 years
- Not pregnant or breast feeding; Both male and female patients must be willing to use effective contraception
- No known hypersensitivity to the excipients of citric acid monohydrate, dihydrate sodium citrate, mannitol, or polysorbate.
L722: ## Treatment
L723: Patients were stratified by ECOG performance status (0 vs 1) and disease stage (recurrent vs metastatic) and randomized 1:1 to Arm A or B.
L724: - Arm AToripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W
- Cisplatin 80 mg/m2 IV Q3W X 6
L725: c. Gemcitabine 1000 mg/m2 IV Days 1, 8 Q3W X 6
L726: ## 2. Arm B
L727: a. Placebo IV Q3W
L728: b. Cisplatin 80 mg/m2 IV Q3W X 6
L729: c. Gemcitabine 1000 mg/m2 IV Days 1, 8 Q3W X 6
L730: - After completing up to 6 cycles of cisplatin/gemcitabine, patients received toripalimab/placebo (depending upon initial assignment) Q3W.
- Patients remained on treatment until disease progression, loss of clinical benefit, death, intolerable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, initiation of new anticancer therapy, or Investigator's decision.
- Patients experiencing clinical benefit (per Investigator) in the absence of unacceptable toxicity or symptomatic deterioration continued treatment with toripalimab/placebo until confirmed disease progression. Crossover of patients in the control arm was not permitted.
L731: ## Monitoring
L732: Routine laboratories were obtained on Days 1 and 8 for the first 6 cycles and then each cycle. Sparse PK sampling and samples for the assessment of ADA were obtained at baseline, every 4 cycles during the 1st year, and every 8 cycles thereafter, and at treatment discontinuation. Thyroid function tests were obtained at baseline, each cycle, and end of treatment. EBV titers were obtained at baseline, every 2 cycles for the first year, then every 3 cycles, and at disease progression. ECGs were obtained at baseline and as clinically indicated. Adverse events were reported from the time of initiation of toripalimab/placebo until 60 days after the last dose and graded according to the National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI-CTCAE) v5.0. Survival follow-up was every 3 months. The EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EORTC-QLQ-H&amp;N35 were obtained at baseline and each cycle.
L733: ## Response Assessments
L734: Imaging of known tumor sites was obtained at baseline, every 6 weeks for 1 year and then every 9 weeks. Imaging included CTs of the nasopharynx, neck, chest and abdomen, with a bone scan if clinically indicated. Baseline imaging included a head CT.
L735: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L736: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design for JUPITER-02. FDA notes that based on version 6 of the protocol, the study was proposed to be conducted in approximately 40 sites in mainland China, Taiwan and Singapore.
L737: ## Study Endpoints
L738: ## The Applicant's Description:
L739: **Primary Endpoint:** The primary endpoint is IRC-determined PFS per RECIST v1.1 in the intent-to-treat (all randomized patients) population.
L740: ## **Secondary Endpoints:**
L741: - IRC-determined ORR and DoR per RECIST v1.1
- OS
- Investigator-determined ORR, DoR, and disease control rate (DCR) per RECIST v1.1, IRC-determined DCR per RECIST v1.1
- Investigator-determined PFS per RECIST v1.1
- Investigator- and IRC-determined PFS rates at 1 and 2 years
- OS rates at 1 and 2 years
- PRO per EORTC QLQ-C30, EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N35, and performance status
- ADA, PK
- Investigator- and IRC-determined PFS, ORR, DoR, and DCR per irRECIST
L742: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L743: ## The Applicant's Description:
L744: ## **Sample Size Estimation**
L745: With 200 PFS events, the study has 80% power to detect a HR = 0.67 with a 2-sided significance level of 0.05 (median PFS 10.4 vs. 7.0 months). One interim PFS analysis was planned when approximately 130 PFS events (65% of events) were seen. The stopping boundary for the interim analysis, 2-sided p-value, was approximately p = 0.011.
L746: The study was not fully powered for OS. At the final OS analysis, with approximately 130 deaths at 5 years, the trial has 46% power to detect an OS improvement with a HR = 0.72 using a 2-sided alpha of 0.05. Two interim OS analyses, for descriptive purposes only, were planned at the interim and final analyses of PFS. At these timepoints, the trial is expected to observe 49 and 74 deaths, respectively.
L747: ## **Primary Efficacy Analysis**
L748: IRC-determined PFS was defined as the time from randomization to the occurrence of IRC-determined disease progression per RECIST v1.1 or death from any cause. Patients who had not progressed at the time of analysis were censored at their last tumor assessment. Patients who died prior to disease progression were considered to have an event. Patients with no post-baseline assessments were censored on the day of randomization and patients who started a new anticancer therapy without IRC-determined PFS were censored at the last tumor assessment prior to the new therapy. Patients with 2 or more consecutive missing assessments prior to progression were censored at the last tumor assessment prior to the missing value. For patients with 2 or more consecutive missing assessments due to COVID-19, if the subsequent assessment was not disease progression, the assessment was
L749: used in the PFS analysis. If the subsequent assessment was disease progression, the patient was censored at the last assessment prior to the missing scans. A sensitivity analysis was also conducted in which all patients with 2 or more consecutive missing assessments (regardless of cause) were censored prior to the missing assessments. The primary efficacy analysis of PFS was conducted in the intent to treat population using a logrank test stratified by ECOG performance status and disease stage (as randomized). The HR was estimated using the stratified Cox proportional hazards model. The Kaplan-Meier method was used to estimate the median PFS. The Brookmeyer Crowley method (Brookmeyer and Crowley, 1982) by applying log-log transformation to survival functions, was used to construct the 95% CI for the median PFS for each treatment arm.
L750: ## Secondary Efficacy Analyses
L751: If the primary endpoint was statistically significant, the secondary endpoints IRC-determined ORR and OS were analyzed hierarchically. Objective response rate was defined as the IRC-determined confirmed complete and partial response rate using RECIST v1.1. Patients not meeting these criteria were considered non-responders. The 95% CI was calculated using the Clopper-Pearson method. The treatment difference in ORR was analyzed using the Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel method. The duration of response was defined as the time interval from the date of the first IRC-determined confirmed CR or PR until the date of IRC-determined progression or death. All IRC evaluations to calculate the duration of response used RECIST v1.1. Censoring rules were identical to those used in the calculation of PFS.
L752: Overall survival was defined as the time from randomization to death from any cause. The methods of censoring and analysis used in the assessment of PFS were used for OS.
L753: ## Additional Analyses
L754: The EORTC QLQ-C30 and EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N35 were scored according to the EORTC scoring manual. If a scale had more than 50% of the items completed, a pro-rated score was computed for that measure. For scales with less than 50% of the items completed, the score was considered missing. The mean, standard deviation, median and range for the absolute scores and the mean (95% CI) changes from baseline were calculated for each subscale at each timepoint. Previously-published minimally important differences were used to identify meaningful changes from baseline.
L755: ## Subgroup Efficacy Analyses
L756: The unstratified hazard ratio was calculated for the following pre-specified subgroups: age \le 50, &gt; 50, gender, ECOG status (0/1), disease stage (recurrent/metastatic), EBV viral copy number, and baseline PD-L1 status.

L763: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L764: FDA agrees with the Applicant's above listing of amendments to the JUPITER-02 Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) table, with the exception that the original SAP version 1.0 was submitted to IND 147826 on June 30, 2020, and not June 30, 2021. The sample size calculation was based on progression-free survival (PFS) which also assumed the accrual duration to be 14 months with an assumed annual drop-out rate of 5%. The trial was expected to enroll 280 patients.
L765: The JUPITER-02 study had a pre-specified testing strategy, provided under version 3.1 of the statistical analysis plan, where ORR was proposed to be tested if PFS was statistically significant,
L766: while OS would be formally tested after 130 deaths were observed. The assumed median OS in the placebo arm was 29 months. Assuming an expected HR for OS as 0.72, the median OS in toripalimab arm would be 40.3 months. In version 3.1 of the statistical analysis plan, the Applicant stated that there would be a two-sided Type I error penalty of 0.000001 for every descriptive interim look of OS, including those requested by regulatory agencies.

L774: ## 8.1.2. POLARIS-02
L775: ## Trial Design
L776: ## The Applicant's Description:
L777: POLARIS-02, is a Phase 2 basket trial with 8 cohorts that included 4 tumor types:
L778: - Gastric and gastro-esophageal junction carcinoma (Cohorts 1 and 5)
L779: - Squamous cell carcinoma of the esophagus (Cohorts 2 and 6)
- Nasopharyngeal carcinoma (Cohorts 3 and 7)
- Squamous cell carcinoma of the head and neck (Cohorts 4 and 8)
L780: The assessment of efficacy for toripalimab monotherapy in POLARIS-02/Cohort 3 will focus on 172 patients who have received prior platinum-based therapy while the assessment of safety will include 190 patients with NPC who received toripalimab and supported by safety data in Cohorts 1, 2, and 4 (as components of the pooled monotherapy dataset).
L781: The assessment of efficacy for toripalimab in combination with chemotherapy is limited to POLARIS-02/Cohort 7, which enrolled 12 patients with NPC who received toripalimab plus cisplatin/gemcitabine. The assessment of safety includes all treated patients in Cohorts 5-8 who received toripalimab plus chemotherapy.
L782: ## Eligibility Criteria Specific for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L783: - Histopathologically and/or cytologically confirmed locally advanced and/or metastatic NPC who meet the following conditions:Disease progression or intolerance to at least 2 lines of treatment (including, but not limited to, chemotherapy or radiochemotherapy) for advanced NPC
- Patients with recurrence or metastasis within 6 months after completion of concomitant adjuvant or neoadjuvant radiochemotherapy are considered to have received 1 prior line of treatment.
- Measurable disease per RECIST v1.1
- ECOG Performance status 0-1
- No necrotic lesions which are thought to be at risk for massive hemorrhage within the 4 weeks prior to study entry
L784: See eligibility criteria for both POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02 above
L785: ## Eligibility Criteria Specific for Cohort 7/POLARIS-02
L786: - Recurrent or metastatic NPC
- No prior systemic chemotherapy for recurrent/metastatic disease
- Completed neoadjuvant/adjuvant chemotherapy &gt; 6 mos prior to entry
L787: See eligibility criteria for both POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02 above
L788: ## Treatment
L789: Cohort 3
L790: ## Toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W
L791: - Patients in Cohort 3 remained on treatment until disease progression, intolerable toxicity, Investigator's decision, patient request for withdrawal, or death.
- Note that patients in Cohorts 1, 2, and 4 received toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W.
L792: ## Cohort 7
L793: - Toripalimab 240/360 mg IV Q3W
- Cisplatin 80 mg/m2 IV Q3W x 6
- Gemcitabine 1000 mg/m2 IV Days 1, 8 Q3W x 6
- Treatment was continued until disease progression, death, withdrawal of informed consent, intolerable toxicity or Investigator's decision.
L794: ## **Monitoring**
L795: Safety parameters included the collection of AEs, laboratories, samples for the assessment of pharmacokinetics and immunogenicity, vital signs, electrocardiograms (ECG), and physical examination. The severity of an AE was evaluated according to NCI-CTCAE v4.03. Adverse events were collected up to 60 days after the last dose of toripalimab. In Cohort 3, routine laboratories were obtained every 2 weeks. In Cohort 7, routine laboratories were obtained every 3 weeks. ECGs were obtained at baseline, Day 1 of each cycle, and at the end of treatment visit. Samples for ADA and pharmacokinetics were obtained at baseline, prior to each cycle x 6 cycles, then every 6 cycles, and at the end of treatment. Nine patients enrolled at a single clinical site across Cohorts 1-4 underwent intensive PK sampling. Twelve patients in Cohorts 5-8 underwent intensive PK sampling during Cycles 1 and 6.
L796: This study also explored the potential correlation between biomarkers (PD-L1 expression in tumor tissues and EBV DNA levels in blood) and the efficacy of toripalimab in patients with advanced NPC.
L797: ## **Response Assessments**
L798: Cohort 3: Tumor imaging was obtained at baseline, every 8 weeks in the first year and every 12 weeks thereafter. Throughout the trial, scans were archived. Scans were assessed by both the Investigator and an Independent Review Committee, using RECIST v1.1 and irRECIST.
L799: Cohort 7: Tumor imaging was obtained at baseline, every 6 weeks in the first year and every 12 weeks thereafter. Throughout the trial, scans were archived. Scans were assessed by the Investigator, using RECIST v1.1 and irRECIST.
L800: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L801: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design for POLARIS-02.
L802: ## **Study Endpoints**
L803: ## The Applicant's Description:
L804: ## **Cohort 3/POLARIS-02**
L805: The primary efficacy endpoint of Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 is IRC-evaluated ORR according to RECIST v1.1.
L806: Secondary efficacy endpoints of Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 include the following:
L807: - Duration of response per IRC per RECIST v1.1
- Time to response (TTR) per IRC
- Disease control rate per IRC
- Progression-free survival per IRC per RECIST v1.1
- Overall survival
- 6-month progression-free survival rate per IRC
- 1-year survival rate
L808: Other secondary endpoints include safety, incidence of ADA, and PK parameters.
L809: Additional analyses of Investigator (INV)-determined ORR and DOR per RECIST v1.1, IRC- and INV-determined ORR and DOR per irRECIST, and IRC- and INV-determined best unconfirmed response per RECIST v1.1 will be conducted.
L810: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L811: In a single arm trial such as POLARIS-02, FDA considers time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS as not interpretable and descriptive only. The efficacy evaluation of this study was based on overall response rate (ORR) and duration of response (DOR).
L812: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L813: ## The Applicant's Description:
L814: ## **Cohort 3/POLARIS-02**
L815: The SAP was prepared after the protocol had been initiated and finalized prior to database lock.
L816: ## **Sample Size Estimation for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02**
L817: Based on the preliminary data in the nasopharyngeal carcinoma cohort, the expected ORR was 24%. Enrollment of approximately 160 patients with progression after two or more prior lines of therapy, including at least one line of platinum-based therapy could provide more than 80% power to ensure the lower limit of the 95% confidential interval of observed ORR (using the Clopper-Pearson method) is no less than 15%. Cohort 3, therefore, enrolled at least 180 patients.
L818: ## **Primary Efficacy Analysis for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02**
L819: The primary efficacy analysis was IRC-determined ORR according to RECIST v1.1. The primary analysis was conducted in the following populations:
L820: - Platinum-Treated Analysis Set (PTAS): All patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC, both keratinizing and nonkeratinizing, with disease progression on or after platinum-containing therapy who received at least one dose of toripalimab.
- Breakthrough-Therapy-Designation Analysis Set (BTDAS): All patients with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing therapy who received at least one dose of toripalimab.
- Full analysis set (FAS): All patients who received at least one dose of toripalimab. This is also referred to as the Safety Set.
L821: The Clopper-Pearson method was used to calculate the 95% confidence interval of ORR.
L822: ## Primary Efficacy Analysis for Cohort 7/POLARIS-02
L823: The primary endpoint is Investigator-determined ORR based on RECIST v1.1.
L824: ## Secondary Endpoints of Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L825: Duration of response was defined as the time from the first recorded IRC-determined response (CR or PR) per RECIST v 1.1 to first recorded IRC-determined progression of disease per RECIST v1.1 or death (whichever comes first). Patients without progression or death were censored on the day of their last tumor assessment. Patients who died or progressed immediately after &gt; 2 consecutive missing tumor assessments were censored at the last post baseline tumor evaluation prior to the missed visits. Patients who started subsequent anticancer therapy without progression were censored at the last tumor evaluation before the new therapy. Duration of response was summarized only for patients who achieved a confirmed CR or PR. The median, Q1 and Q3 of DoR were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method and the two-sided 95% CI for the median DoR was estimated using the Brookmeyer-Crowley method (Brookmeyer and Crowley, 1982).
L826: Time to response was defined as the time from the first dose to the first recorded IRC-determined response (CR or PR) per RECIST v1.1. It was calculated only for patients with a confirmed response. The Kaplan-Meier method was used to estimate the median event time, Q1 and Q3.
L827: The disease control rate was defined as the proportion of patients with IRC-determined confirmed CR, PR, or SD per RECIST v1.1. The Clopper-Pearson method was used to calculate the 95% confidence interval.
L828: Progression-free survival was defined as the time from first dose to first recorded IRC-determined progression of disease per RECIST v1.1 or death due to any cause (whichever comes first). Patients without an event were censored at the last tumor evaluation. Patients with no post-baseline
L829: evaluation were censored on the day of the first dose. Patients who died or progressed immediately after &gt; 2 consecutive missing tumor assessments were censored at the last post baseline tumor evaluation prior to the missed visits. Patients without a post baseline tumor evaluation prior to the missed visits were censored on the day of the first dose. Patients who started subsequent anticancer therapy without progression were censored at the last tumor evaluation before the new therapy.
L830: OS was defined as the time from the first dose to death due to any cause. Patients without an event were censored on the last known survival date. Patients with no follow-up information were censored on the day of first dose.
L831: 6M PFS% refers to the proportion of subjects without progression or death within 6 months after the first dose. 1YR OS% refers to the proportion of subjects who survive at least 1 year after the first dose.
L832: ## Secondary Endpoints for Cohort 7/POLARIS-02
L833: Secondary endpoints included Investigator-determined DOR, TTR, DCR, PFS, 6M PFS% per RECIST v1.1, Investigator-determined ORR and DoR per irRECIST, OS, 1 YR OS%, safety, PK and immunogenicity
L834: ## Additional Endpoints for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L835: Investigator-determined ORR and DoR per RECIST v1.1 as well as both IRC- and INV-determined ORR and DoR per irRECIST were assessed.
L836: ## Subgroup Efficacy Analyses for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L837: Subgroup analyses were performed to assess the consistency and robustness of the primary efficacy endpoint using: age, gender, ethnicity, prior lines of treatment, prior radiotherapy, histological subtype, baseline metastases, number of involved organs at baseline, ECOG performance status at baseline, liver metastases at baseline, stage at entry, PD-L1 status, EBV antibody, EBV DNA viral load, MSI expression, time since last treatment, time since last chemotherapy, and time since last non-chemotherapy.
L838: The SAP was modified to be consistent with the requirements of the National Medical Products Administration (China) and later to be consistent with the agreements with the FDA.

L844: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L845: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L846: Initially, at least 1 prior therapy for NPC was required in Cohort 3. Based on the substantial anti-tumor activity seen with NPC, the protocol was amended (Version 5.0) to increase the sample size and to include patients who had received at least 2 prior lines of therapy for advanced NPC. Prior to Amendment 5, scans from all patients were archived. Amendment 5 modified the primary endpoint to be IRC-determined response rate per RECIST v1.1 and all scans were reviewed by the IRC.

L851: ## 8.1.3. Study Results
L852: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L853: ## Data:
L854: The clinical studies presented in this submission were conducted in accordance with the ethical principles that have their origin in the Declaration of Helsinki and are consistent with the International Council for Harmonization guidelines for Good Clinical Practice and applicable regulatory requirements. The original protocols and protocol amendments were approved by an independent Institutional Review Board/Ethics Committee associated with each study center. Signed Informed consent was obtained from all participants prior to any study-specific procedures.
L855: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L856: POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02 were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice and provides suitable data for review in accordance with 21 CFR 312.120 and 314.106.
L857: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L858: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L859: ## **Financial Disclosure**
L860: ## **Data:**
L861: In accordance with 21 CFR 54, the Applicant submitted financial disclosure certification documents for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 on June 25, 2021 and for JUPITER-02 in the final submission of the rolling BLA in Module 1.3.4. The documents include the list of all investigators who participated in POLARIS-02 or JUPITER-02 by study site. No Investigator on either trial had a reportable financial disclosure prior to the initiation of the study. In addition, no Investigator reported a change of financial interests during the study.
L862: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L863: None of the Investigators for POLARIS-02 or JUPITER-02 had a reportable financial disclosure.
L864: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L865: The Applicant indicated above that the JUPITER-02 financial disclosure certification documents were in the final submission of the rolling BLA in Module 1.3.4. For clarity, the date of the submission was August 31, 2021. FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. See Section 20.2 Financial Disclosure for additional information.
L866: ## **Patient Disposition**
L867: ## **Data:**
L868: JUPITER-02 was conducted from October 2018 until May 2020 at 35 sites in mainland China, Taiwan, and Singapore. POLARIS-02 was conducted from December 2016 to February 2020 at 20 sites in mainland China. The table below provides information on the disposition of patients in JUPITER-02
L869: and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02. This is followed by information on the disposition of patients on Cohort 7/POLARIS-02 in text below the table.
L870: In JUPITER-02, the median follow-up was 10.7 months. Among the 289 patients randomized and treated on the trial, 144 (49.8%) patients have discontinued treatment including 56 who discontinued during the chemotherapy phase (31 toripalimab, 25 placebo) and 88 post-chemotherapy (24 toripalimab, 64 placebo). All efficacy and safety analyses were conducted in 289 patients.
L871: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the median follow-up was 12.7 months. Analyses were conducted in the following three populations:
L872: - Platinum-Treated Analysis Set (PTAS): All patients with recurrent or metastatic keratinizing or non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing therapy who received at least one dose of toripalimab.
- Breakthrough-Therapy-Designation Analysis Set (BTDAS): All patients with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing therapy who received at least one dose of toripalimab.
- Full Analysis Set (FAS): All patients who received at least one dose of toripalimab. This is also referred to as the Safety Set.
L873: Only the analyses based on the PTAS and the Safety Set are included in the assessment aid for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02. Efficacy analyses are conducted in the PTAS while safety analyses are conducted in the Safety Set.

L883: ## The Applicant's Position:
L884: In JUPITER-02 and in Cohorts 3 and 7 of POLARIS-02, the most common reason for treatment discontinuation was disease progression, with few deaths prior to disease progression. It is likely that, in POLARIS-02, general health deterioration represents clinical disease progression. The number of patients who discontinued due to an AE or withdrew from the trial was low.
L885: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L886: With the safety update, TopAlliance provided an updated data cutoff date of June 8, 2021, (original application data cutoff date of May 30, 2020) for JUPITER-02. For POLARIS-02, the primary efficacy population consists of only those patients previously treated with platinum-based chemotherapy, and so patient disposition was only verified by FDA for this population.
L887: The Applicant provided data on the 12 patients in Cohort 7/POLARIS-02 in the application and clarified there were 3 patients who received toripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine and 9 patients who received toripalimab 360 mg IV Q3W in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine. Due to the small number of patients who received toripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, FDA did not include this population in the FDA analyses.
L888: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L889: ## Data:
L890: In POLARIS-02, protocol deviations were categorized as critical, major, and minor. Critical protocol deviations, which occurred in 27 (14.2%) patients included failure to submit information on SAEs to the Applicant within the protocol-mandated timeframes; receipt of prohibited anti-cancer treatment during the screening or treatment period; and failure to obtain baseline screening laboratory tests for safety. The use of other investigational drugs was not allowed, and the use of traditional Chinese
L891: medicine was considered a protocol violation if anticancer activities were specified in the labeling for that traditional Chinese medicine. Four patients took prohibited concomitant medications. None of these patients responded to toripalimab. Major protocol deviations, which occurred in 107 (56.3%) patients, included failure to obtain informed consent or re-consent using an updated informed consent document, failure to obtain protocol-specified safety laboratory tests or obtaining them outside the protocol-specified window, failure to conduct testing for clinically significant laboratory abnormalities as specified in the protocol and use of prohibited medications. See Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 Table 14.1.1.2.3.
L892: In JUPITER-02, there were 120 (41.5%) patients with a major protocol deviation. This includes 24 patients (13 toripalimab and 11 placebo) with a major protocol deviation due to COVID-19. Other major protocol deviations were procedural deviation (N=70, 39 toripalimab, 31 placebo), treatment compliance deviation (N=33, 12 toripalimab, 21 placebo), Other (N=29, 13 toripalimab, 16 placebo), failure to meet the eligibility criteria (N=16, 5 toripalimab, 11 placebo), prohibited therapy (N=2, 0 toripalimab, 2 placebo), and withdrawal criteria deviation (N=2, 0 toripalimab, 2 placebo). Among the 24 patients with major protocol deviations due to COVID-19, the vast majority of these were due to missing (usually 1 assessment) imaging assessments due to COVID-19. See JUPITER-02 Table 14.1.2.
L893: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L894: The majority of protocol deviations were deemed unlikely to have an impact on the assessment of efficacy or safety by the Investigators and the Applicant. In POLARIS-02, none of the 4 patients with critical protocol deviations due to use of a prohibited concomitant medications responded to toripalimab; thus these deviations had no impact on the assessment of efficacy. With regard to JUPITER-02, the majority of the major protocol deviations due to COVID-19 were due to missing tumor assessments (usually a single assessment).
L895: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L896: FDA verified the Applicant's three categories (i.e., critical, major, and minor) to classify the protocol deviations in the 190 patients in POLARIS-02 providing primary support for the previously treated NPC indication. FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the critical protocol deviations in POLARIS-02. The Applicant referenced the clinical study report of POLARIS-02 for the major protocol deviations; a patient could be counted in more than one protocol deviation. In the 190 patients, there were 19% failure to obtain informed consent or re-consent using an updated informed consent document, 17% failure to complete visit schedule criteria within the specified visit window, 15% failure to either administer toripalimab within the specified period or did not follow the recommended storage conditions, 12% failure to conduct testing for clinically significant laboratory abnormalities as specified in the protocol, 11% failure to follow study procedure criteria (e.g., not performing ECG test, HBV test, or physical exams at study visit), 6% administrative criteria (e.g., sub-
L897: investigator not being documented), 3.2% eligibility and entry criteria (e.g. during screening period, anti-tumor drugs should not be used within 4 weeks prior to first dose of toripalimab), and 2.1% use of prohibited medications.
L898: The Applicant did not implement a protocol amendment because of the ongoing COVID-19 pandemic for the JUPITER-02 clinical trial and FDA agrees with the Applicant's identification of 24 patients with major protocol deviations (i.e., missing tumor assessment).
L899: The ongoing COVID-19 pandemic did not impact Cohort 3 in the POLARIS-02 clinical trial, as this cohort was fully enrolled and the data cut-off date was February 19, 2020, which is also the date of last patient completed 1-year follow-up.
L900: While the overall incidence of protocol deviations was relatively high, FDA agrees that these protocol deviations are not expected to have a meaningful impact on the assessment of efficacy or safety for either JUPITER-02 or POLARIS.

This is consistent with the known demographic profile for patients with NPC. Note that since performance status was one of the stratification factors for JUPITER-02, the performance status in the table below is per the IWRS; the performance status per CRF provides discrepant results for only one patient.

L911: ## The Applicant's Position
L912: The baseline demographic characteristics across studies are similar. Patients in both studies are relatively young with a male predominance, which is consistent with the demographics of patients with NPC worldwide and in the US. In the US, the median age of all patients with NPC is 52.7 years and 70.9% are male (Ou et al., 2007).
L913: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L914: FDA agrees with the Applicant's results for the patient characteristics in JUPITER-02 and for the population of patients previously treated with platinum-based chemotherapy from Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02. For the study population of 289 patients enrolled in JUPITER-02, population characteristics were: median age 48 years (range 19 to 72), 4.8% age 65 years or older, 83% male, 100% Asian, and 57% had ECOG PS 0.
L915: FDA agrees that regarding male predominance, this is consistent with what is expected in the US patient population with NPC. However, the median age of patients in JUPITER-02 (48 years) and Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02 (45 years) is younger than the median age for US patients with NPC (53 or 54 years). Furthermore, given that all patients in the efficacy populations for both studies were of Chinese ethnicity, the efficacy populations do not reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population with NPC, and patients with keratinizing histology are underrepresented in these studies relative to the incidence (17-25%) of this histology in the US patient population with NPC.

L927: ## Other Baseline Characteristics
L928: ## Data:
L929: The table below provides the baseline disease characteristics of the patients in JUPITER-02. This is followed by a table that provides the baseline characteristics of patients on Cohort 3/ POLARIS-02 and then by information on the 12 patients in Cohort 7/POLARIS-02.
L930: JUPITER-02 included both patients who presented with metastatic disease and enrolled directly on the trial and those with localized disease who had received prior therapy and developed recurrent disease. At study entry, 59.5% (85 toripalimab, 87 placebo) had recurrent disease, and of these 172 patients who had recurrent disease, 70.3% (59 toripalimab, 62 placebo) had received prior concurrent platinum-based chemoradiation.
L931: Disease stage (recurrent/metastatic), one of the stratification factors, is reported per IWRS in the table below. In the IWRS, 83 and 63 patients in the toripalimab arm and 82 and 61 in the placebo arm were reported to have recurrent or metastatic disease, respectively. In the CRFs, 85 and 61 patients in the toripalimab arm and 87 and 56 in the placebo arm had recurrent or metastatic
L932: disease, respectively. Despite these differences the arms were well balanced for disease stage as well as other baseline disease characteristics.

L946: In Cohort 7/POLARIS-02, all 12 patients had metastatic disease. Liver metastases were present in 8 patients. Nine patients had received at least 1 prior systemic therapy and 10 had received prior radiotherapy. Finally, among the 12 patients, 9 were PD-L1 high (tumor proportion score &gt; 1% by immunohistochemistry using the SP142 antibody in a clinical trial assay) and 3 were PD-L1 low.
L947: ## The Applicant's Position:
L948: The population treated in JUPITER-02 and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 were managed according to the standard of care for patients with NPC in the US. In JUPITER-02, the majority of patients had metastatic disease; 40.8% had liver metastases. Prior to study entry, concurrent chemoradiation was administered to 70.3% of those with recurrent disease. In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the patient population was heavily pretreated and representative of patients undergoing later-line therapy for NPC. Patients had received a median of 4 prior therapies and liver metastases were present in 56.4% of patient in the PTAS. All patients in the PTAS had received prior platinum-based therapy, the majority (91.3%) received cisplatin.
L949: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L950: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position, with the exception of median lines of therapy. Per FDA analyses (refer to FDA Table 16), the median number of systemic therapies for the efficacy population of from Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02 (n=172) was 2 (range 1-13). It appears the Applicant's estimates of prior therapies may have included localized therapy such as radiation, which could explain this difference. Among the 289 patients in JUPITER-02, 86% had metastatic disease at study entry, while in the efficacy population of 172 patients from Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02, 95% had metastatic disease. Please refer to FDA Tables 15 and 16 for the summary of additional clinically relevant disease characteristics for JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02, respectively.
L951: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L952: ## Data:
L953: ## **Treatment Compliance**
L954: Toripalimab was administered intravenously, and each infusion was documented. Patients did interrupt dosing due to AEs, as discussed below.
L955: ## **Concomitant Medications**
L956: On all trials, the use of traditional Chinese medications was not considered a protocol violation. However, the use of traditional Chinese medications with anticancer activity clearly specified in the package insert or other investigational medication was not allowed.
L957: In JUPITER-02, 88.9% of patients received a form of granulocyte colony stimulating factor (87.0% toripalimab, 90.9% placebo), 37.7% IL-11 (35.6% toripalimab, 39.9% placebo), and 28.0% thrombopoietin (28.8% toripalimab, 27.3% placebo). Concomitant radiation therapy was administered to one patient in each arm.
L958: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the most frequently used concomitant medications were levothyroxine (23.7%), zoledronic acid (17.9%), celecoxib (15.8%), lactulose (12.1%), and oxycodone (12.1%).
L959: Among the 60 patients enrolled on Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02 (including Cohort 7), concomitant medications included granulocyte colony stimulating factor (46.7%) and IL-11 (23.3%).
L960: ## Rescue Medication Use
L961: The use of corticosteroids in patients who developed immune-related adverse events is discussed below in the section on immune-related adverse events.
L962: ## The Applicant's Position:
L963: The use concomitant medications did not impact the efficacy or safety of toripalimab.
L964: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L965: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA did not perform an analysis on the 60 patients enrolled on Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02.
L966: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L967: ## Data:
L968: ## JUPITER-02
L969: The pre-specified interim PFS analysis of JUPITER-02, at 64% of the information fraction, demonstrated a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in IRC-determined PFS. This analysis used the stratification factors obtained at the time of randomization (per Interactive Web Response System (IWRS)) and the results are shown in the table below. The study crossed the O'Brien-Fleming boundary for this interim analysis (two-sided 0.010, updated based on the 128 events actually observed) with a p = 0.0003. These results were very similar to the per protocol analysis which had a HR of 0.51 (95% CI: 0.35, 0.73).

L976: CT15 Table 14.2.2.1
L977: Figure 18: IRC-determined PFS
L978: In the analysis above, patients who missed 2 or more tumor assessments due to the COVID-19 pandemic were not censored if their subsequent scan did not show progressive disease. However, patients who missed 2 or more tumor assessments due to COVID-19 and whose subsequent scan showed progressive disease were censored at their last assessment prior to the missing scans. Using this method, 8 patients in the toripalimab and 5 in the placebo arm were censored prior to 2 or more missing scans. In a sensitivity analysis in which patients who missed 2 or more assessments were censored at the last assessment prior to the missing scans (10 patients toripalimab and 7 placebo), the HR was 0.51 (95% CI: 0.36, 0.73).
L979: An additional sensitivity analysis was conducted in which patients who missed 2 or more consecutive assessments (regardless of cause) and who died or had progressive disease on a subsequent scan were considered to have an event (HR = 0.53, 95% CI: 0.38, 0.76). A further analysis was conducted in which the stratification factors on the case report forms (rather than the IWRS) were used. The HR for this analysis was 0.50 (95% CI: 0.35, 0.72). The analysis of the Investigator-determined PFS had a HR of 0.41 (95% CI: 0.28, 0.59).
L980: The HR for the IRC-determined PFS in the per protocol population was 0.51 (95% CI: 0.35, 0.73). With 133 events, median Investigator-determined PFS was not evaluable in the toripalimab and 8.0 months in the placebo arm; HR = 0.41 (95% CI: 0.28, 0.59).
L981: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L982: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of the results of the primary endpoint of PFS for JUPITER-02 study. PFS was statistically significant at the interim analysis (128 events, 64% information fraction) with a p-value of 0.0003 compared to the alpha bound of 0.010 based on the observed information fraction. The expected pre-specified IF for the interim analysis was 65% (130 events); however, the difference in number of events (2) is unlikely to impact the statistical inference of the interim analysis.
L983: The following table provides the exploratory subgroup analyses of PFS per IRC in JUPITER-02. In general, the results are consistent in favoring the toripalimab arm across almost all subgroups, with the exception of the group of patients aged 65 years and older which is very small in sample size. Since the trial enrolled only Asian patients in China, subgroups by race and region were not applicable.
L984: Given the nominal number of patients belonging to the histology subtypes of “keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma”, “unclassified nasopharyngeal carcinoma” and “other” (3, 3 and 1, respectively) in both treatment arms, exploratory analyses were not conducted in these subgroups.

L990: ## Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L991: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the IRC-determined confirmed response rate per RECIST v1.1 was the primary endpoint and duration of response was a key secondary endpoint. The response rates in the PTAS and in the Safety Set were accompanied by a prolonged median duration of response of greater than 1 year. The median DoR was calculated using the Kaplan-Meier method and these results differ from the proportion of responding patients at 1 year. The PTAS included 5 patients with keratinizing NPC that were excluded from the BTDA. Three of these 5 (60.0%, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 14.7, 94.7) patients had an IRC-determined response to toripalimab.

L997: The Investigator-determined response rates and durations of response were comparable to those of the IRC. ORR was 20.9% and 21.1% in the PTAS and Safety Set, respectively. The Investigator-determined median DoR was 12.8 months in the PTAS and 12.1 months in the Safety Set. Among the 7 patients who developed anti-drug antibodies (ADA) in the PTAS, the ORR was 28.6%. This can be compared to an ORR of 20.6% among the 165 patients without ADA.
L998: ## **Cohort 7/POLARIS-02**
L999: In Cohort 7/POLARIS-02, the Investigator-determined objective response rate per RECIST v1.1 among the 12 patients was 75.0% (95% CI: 42.8, 94.5). The median duration of response was 7.1 months (95% CI: 4.2, 15.3).
L1000: In addition to the information available from POLARIS-02, 9 patients with NPC were treated with toripalimab on CT1, CT3, or TAB001-01. Among these patients, there was 1 partial response.
L1001: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1002: In JUPITER-02, treatment with toripalimab plus cisplatin/gemcitabine significantly decreased the risk of disease progression or death by 48% (HR=0.52; 95% CI: 0.359, 0.740; p=0.0003). This difference was clinically meaningful with a 3.7 month difference in median PFS, 11.7 versus 8.0 months. Sensitivity analyses and the Investigator-determined PFS were consistent with the primary analysis.
L1003: A response rate of 75.0% was seen among the 12 patients with NPC in Cohort 7/POLARIS-02 treated with toripalimab, cisplatin, and gemcitabine. This is similar to the 80.8% response rate seen in the JUPITER-02 trial.
L1004: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, toripalimab demonstrated substantial activity and a prolonged duration of response in a group of heavily pre-treated patients. The IRC-determined response rates in both the
L1005: PTAS and Safety Set were accompanied by a median DoR of greater than 1 year. The PTAS included 5 patients with keratinizing NPC. Three of these 5 (60.0%, 95% CI: 14.7, 94.7) patients had an IRC-determined response to toripalimab. While the number of patients is small, the result suggests that toripalimab also has substantial activity in patients with the keratinized subtype of NPC.
L1006: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1007: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of the results of the endpoints of ORR and DOR as assessed by IRC per RECIST 1.1 in Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02. The following table provides the results of exploratory subgroup analyses of ORR by PD-L1 expression (TPS \ge 1% vs. TPS &lt; 1%). The response rates and the observed DOR rate at 6 months appear to be consistent across PD-L1 expression levels. There were seven patients with missing PD-L1 status; those patients were not included in the exploratory subgroup analysis by PD-L1 expression.

L1018: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1019: ## Data:
L1020: All the required components of the electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) were included in the submission. All studies were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice. Case Report Forms were reviewed and compared with the submitted datasets and analyses produced using the datasets. No data quality or integrity issues were identified from JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02. See Section 4.1 above concerning the clinical site inspections.
L1021: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1022: POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02 were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice with close attention to data quality and integrity and provide suitable data for review in accordance with 21 CFR 312.120 and 314.106.
L1023: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1024: FDA did not identify any issues related to data quality and integrity of the datasets submitted to support the BLA. See also Section 6.2.2.2 Compliance with GCP and Section 4.1. The RRA did not find significant concerns regarding GCP compliance. On-site inspections had not yet been conducted at the time of regulatory action.
L1025: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1026: ## **Data:**
L1027: ## **JUPITER-02**
L1028: The IRC-determined response rates in the toripalimab and placebo arms according to RECIST v1.1 are shown in the table below. The difference in response rates was statistically significant. The Investigator-determined ORRs were 80.8% and 74.8% and the median DoRs were not evaluable and 5.8 months in the toripalimab and placebo arms, respectively. Further, the IRC-determined ORR in the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02 was similar to the 75.0% ORR seen in Cohort 7/POLARIS-02. Among the 5 patients who developed ADA on the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02, the ORR was 60.0%. This can be compared to an ORR of 78.0% in the 141 without ADA.
L1029: The OS results from JUPITER-02 were not mature as of the cut-off date. The median survival was not reached in either arm. The 1-year OS rates were 91.2% and 88.4% in the toripalimab and placebo arms, respectively. With 29 events, the first interim analysis of OS on JUPITER-02 had a HR = 0.78 (95% CI: 0.37, 1.64). An ad-hoc analysis of overall survival was done in preparation for a presentation at ASCO 2021 and for a publication (Mai et al., 2021).

L1042: ## **Cohort 3/POLARIS-02**
L1043: The median IRC-determined PFS in the PTAS and Safety Set was 1.9 months with a 6-month PFS of 29.1% and 30.8%, in the PTAS and Safety Set, respectively. Median OS was 15.7 months in the PTAS and 17.4 months in the Safety Set.
L1044: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1045: In JUPITER-02, survival data is immature, but the HR favors toripalimab. The significant improvement in response rate and prolonged duration of response in the toripalimab arm are consistent with this improvement in PFS. Note that the response rate in the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02 is similar to the response rate seen in Cohort 7/POLARIS-02. In POLARIS-02, toripalimab has demonstrated a substantial response rate and a prolonged duration of response in a group of heavily pre-treated patients with NPC. The median OS is consistent with (and slightly better than) that reported in the literature for patients with NPC who have received prior platinum therapy (Ngeow et al., 2011; Ma et al., 2018).

L1055: As the primary endpoint of PFS was statistically significant, the hierarchical testing plan allowed for the testing of the secondary endpoint of ORR with the same alpha level. Given that ORR was statistically significant, the next endpoint to be tested in the statistical analysis plan will be OS, which will be tested after 130 deaths. The efficacy boundary for the final OS analysis will be adjusted based on the number of interim looks for OS. The current results of OS analysis indicate a trend towards a survival benefit for the toripalimab arm as compared to the placebo arm. However, the total number of OS events at the data cut-off dated June 8, 2021, were small, and the results are not robust. Thus, no meaningful conclusions can be drawn based on these OS results.

L1068: Regarding the Applicant's presentation of additional secondary endpoint results from POLARIS-02, time to event endpoints, such as PFS and OS, are not considered interpretable in a single arm study such as POLARIS-02. Thus, these analyses were not included in FDA's review.
L1069: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1070: ## Data:
L1071: Dose ranging studies have evaluated the following doses of toripalimab, 0.3, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg IV Q2W. In vitro, concentrations \ge 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} toripalimab achieve complete PD-1 receptor occupancy on the surface of circulating T-cells obtained during Phase 1 clinical studies. To ensure complete receptor occupancy in the tumor microenvironment, a serum/plasma concentration of at least 20 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was chosen for future trials. Toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W was selected as the recommended Phase 2 dose based on steady state trough levels of 20-40 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The choice of a fixed dose of toripalimab, 240 mg IV Q3W was based on PK findings that the C_{\text{Min}} of toripalimab at this dose and schedule were similar to those seen at 3 mg/kg IV Q2W. The maintenance of this C_{\text{Min}} level ensures receptor occupancy, with either 3 mg/kg IV Q2W or 240 mg IV Q3W, throughout the dosing interval.
L1072: The efficacy exposure-response analysis of the data from JUPITER-02 will be submitted after the fourth and final rolling submission for this BLA. The efficacy E-R analysis for Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 was conducted in 161 patients with both PK and response information (Figure 13). This analysis was based on 36 responders. The C_{\text{ave}} was derived from the last dose to achieve the "highest" exposure. Logistic regression was conducted to describe the relationship between toripalimab exposure and objective tumor response after adjusting for the effect of other significant covariates.
L1073: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1074: Dose-ranging studies were conducted in a heterogeneous population of patients with solid tumors. An MTD was not identified and there were no apparent dose-response relationships. The recommended dose was selected based on a pharmacodynamic approach to achieve trough concentration predicted to ensure complete PD-1 receptor occupancy in the tumor microenvironment.
L1075: Exposure-response analyses appears to show a relationship between C_{\text{ave}} and response. However, this analysis was based on 36 patients and responses were observed in all 4 exposure quartiles. Further, the majority of the data in the lowest and highest quartiles was imputed. Due to the small number of patients, the exposure-response relationship needs to be verified with additional data from JUPITER-02.
L1076: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1077: Refer to FDA Assessment in Section 20.4.2.
L1078: ## Durability of Response
L1079: ## Data:
L1080: In JUPITER-02, the median PFS in the toripalimab arm increased by 3.7 months and the median duration of response increased by 4.3 months compared to those in the placebo arm. The median duration of response exceeds 1 year in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 and over 80% of patients had a duration of response of at least 6 months.
L1081: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1082: In a group of patients with no prior systemic therapy for recurrent or metastatic disease, the PFS in patients treated with toripalimab in combination with chemotherapy was prolonged in patients treated with toripalimab. In a heavily pre-treated group of patients with few/no treatment options in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the responses observed in patients treated with toripalimab are prolonged in duration (median DOR 14.9 months in the PTAS) .
L1083: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1084: The median duration of response in JUPITER-02 was 10 months in the toripalimab arm and 5.7 months in the placebo arm. The observed 6-month landmark rates of DOR were 44% in toripalimab arm, as compared to 28% in the placebo arm. L1150: ## 8.1.6. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1151: ## **Data:**
L1152: ## **JUPITER-02**
L1153: - JUPITER-02 randomized patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC who had not received prior therapy for recurrent/metastatic disease to toripalimab with cisplatin/gemcitabine (N = 146) or placebo plus cisplatin/gemcitabine (N = 143).The primary endpoint, IRC-determined PFS showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS. The median PFS was 11.7 months in the toripalimab and 8.0 months in the placebo arm; HR 0.52, p = 0.0003.
- This was supported by an analysis of the secondary endpoints, response rate and duration of response. Response rates were 77.4% and 66.4% in the toripalimab and placebo arms respectively, p = 0.03 and were accompanied by a median duration of response of 10.0 months in the toripalimab and 5.7 months in the placebo arm.
- Among the three patients with keratinizing disease, one patient in the toripalimab arm responded to study drugs.
- Among the 5 patients who developed ADA on toripalimab, ORR was 60%. This can be compared to an ORR of 78% in the 141 without ADA.
L1154: ## **Cohort 7/POLARIS-02**
L1155: - Cohort 7/POLARIS-02 administered toripalimab in combination with cisplatin/gemcitabine to patients receiving first-line treatment for NPC (N = 12).Investigator-determined objective response rate per RECIST v1.1 was 75.0% (95% CI: 42.8, 94.5). The median duration of response was 7.1 months (95% CI: 4.2, 15.3).
L1156: ## Cohort 3/POLARIS-02
L1157: - Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 administered toripalimab monotherapy to a heavily pre-treated group of patients with locally advanced/metastatic NPC who had received prior platinum-based therapy (N = 172).In the PTAS, the ORR was 20.9% (95% CI: 15.1, 27.8) and the median duration of response was 14.9 months (95% CI: 10.3, NE).
- The PTAS included 5 patients with keratinizing NPC. While the number of patients is small, 3 of these 5 (60.0%, 95% CI: 14.7, 94.7) patients had an IRC-determined response to toripalimab.
- Among the 7 patients who developed ADA, ORR was 28.6%. This can be compared to an ORR of 20.6% among the 165 patients without ADA.
L1158: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1159: In the US, there are no approved drugs for the treatment of NPC and there are an insufficient number of patients in the US to support the conduct of a trial that is adequately powered to establish safety and efficacy of a drug. Cisplatin in combination with gemcitabine is considered (per National Comprehensive Cancer Network guidelines) the optimal first-line treatment for patients with recurrent locally advanced or metastatic NPC (Zhang et al., 2016; Hong et al, 2020). Patients who progress on or after platinum-based therapies, again, have no FDA-approved treatment options and generally receive off-label, single-agent treatment. Agents which have demonstrated activity, but a limited duration of response in small single-arm trials include taxanes, gemcitabine, irinotecan, and capecitabine/5-fluorouracil (Table 2).
L1160: Given the rarity of NPC in the US, to conduct a large clinical trial, it is necessary to recruit all or at least the vast majority of patients from Southeast Asia. Further, an international trial involving US sites would include only a small number of patients from the US. POLARIS-02 was conducted solely in China while the confirmatory trial JUPITER-02 was conducted in China, Taiwan, and Singapore. Both were adequate and well-controlled trials that demonstrated internal consistency and were conducted under GCP (FDAMA Section 115(a), Guidance for Industry, December 2019), as set out in 21 CFR 314.106 and in compliance with the requirements of 21 CFR 312.120. The BLA follows the eCTD format and the content and format of documents follows relevant ICH guidelines and guidance provided in the Guidance for Industry and FDA Staff FDA Acceptance of Foreign Clinical Studies Not Conducted Under an IND Frequently Asked Questions (March 2012).
L1161: The BLA contains information required under 21 CFR 314.106, including 314.106(b)(1), that these data are applicable to the US population and US medical practice. Specifically, as discussed further below, the natural history, diagnostic evaluation, medical management and treatment are similar between the US and China and any minor differences in disease characteristics would not be predicted to affect the efficacy or safety observed in JUPITER-02 and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 based on
L1162: population pharmacokinetic modeling and subgroup analyses. Furthermore, the pharmacokinetic profile of toripalimab meets the criteria described in ICH E5(R1), of a drug that, based its the metabolic pathway, wide therapeutic index and flat exposure response for safety, is unlikely to be sensitive to racial/ethnic differences. This is further supported by published literature showing that, as a class, the pharmacokinetics of monoclonal antibodies are generally not sensitive to ethnic differences.
L1163: The data obtained from JUPITER-02 and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 show substantial, confirmed benefit for the use of toripalimab in the treatment of NPC. Further supportive evidence of the effectiveness of toripalimab is provided by the top-line results of additional randomized, placebo-controlled studies demonstrating statistically significant improvements in PFS and, for ESCC, improvements in OS with the addition of toripalimab to standard chemotherapy.
L1164: The results of JUPITER-02 and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 are applicable to the US population and US medical practice (21 CFR 314.106), taking into account the following information.
L1165: - **Natural history:** In both China and the US, the majority of patients present with nonkeratinizing NPC. In Australia, the outcome of NPC, when corrected for histology, was very similar in Asian and non-Asian patients (Corry et al., 2006). Further, in a matched control trial, there was no difference in disease-specific survival by race (Bhattacharyya, 2004; Sun et al., 2007). While the natural history of keratinizing and nonkeratinizing disease differs, when corrected for histology, the natural history of NPC is very similar between China and Western nations (Vazquez et al., 2014).

- **Treatment:** The treatment of keratinizing and nonkeratinizing NPC is identical in the US and both have a dismal prognosis following the development of recurrent or metastatic disease. Treatment standard for patients with NPC in the US and China have been very similar and the American and Chinese Societies of Clinical Oncology (ASCO and CSCO) have recently issued joint guidelines for the treatment of Stage II-IVA NPC (Chen et al., 2021). The National Comprehensive Cancer Network and the CSCO guidelines for the treatment of patients with Stage IVB (metastatic) disease are very similar. Both recommend initial treatment with cisplatin/gemcitabine (preferred) or a platinum taxane regimen (NCCN guidelines, 2021; CSCO guidelines, 2020).

- **Demographics:** The demographic characteristics of the patients treated on JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02 are similar to those of patients with NPC in the US. The median age of patients on POLARIS-02 who received prior platinum-based therapy was 45 years and 83.1% were male while on JUPITER-02, the median age was 48 years and 83.0% of patients were male. In the US, the median age of all patients with NPC is 52.7 years and 70.9% are male (Ou et al., 2007). Importantly, there was no correlation between age and toripalimab exposure.
L1166: - Exposure: *In vitro*, toripalimab concentrations \ge 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} achieve complete PD-1 receptor occupancy of T-cells in the peripheral blood. To ensure complete receptor occupancy in the tumor microenvironment, a toripalimab dose capable of achieving serum/plasma concentrations of 20 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} was chosen for clinical development. Toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W and 240 mg IV Q3W both achieve trough levels above 20 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} and these two trials provide evidence of safety and effectiveness with both treatment regimens.Population PK modeling based on data from the toripalimab monotherapy population demonstrated similar PK parameters for toripalimab at 3 mg/kg IV Q2W and 240 mg IV Q3W with the following steady state values: C_{\text{trough}} 35.6 and 24.1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, AUC 18868 and 24194 h \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, and C_{\text{Max}} 92.3 and 95.6 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, respectively.
- Population PK analysis of patients who received toripalimab monotherapy showed a 22% relative reduction in the volume of distribution in Whites when compared to Asians with no difference in clearance based on race, which is less clinically meaningful for a monoclonal antibody than for small molecules. This finding is expected to result in higher exposure to toripalimab in Whites than Asians. This difference is unlikely to have an effect on efficacy (there is complete receptor occupancy at concentrations 2-fold lower than trough levels) or safety (there is a flat exposure-safety relationship for toripalimab over the dose range studied in clinical trials).
- Prior anticancer therapies: The prior anticancer therapies administered to patients on JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02 are consistent with the practice patterns in the US. A substantial percentage of the patients with recurrent disease on JUPITER-02 (70.3%) had received prior platinum-based chemoradiation therapy. Patients in the PTAS population of POLARIS-02 all received platinum-based therapy (the majority received cisplatin) and were heavily pre-treated with a median of 4 prior therapies (range; 1-15).
L1167: The application contains data supporting two indications for the treatment of NPC. Given the estimated 5-year survival rate of patients with metastatic NPC in the US, a substantial proportion of patients with NPC will have already received first-line therapy for recurrent, locally advanced or metastatic NPC and this unmet need will continue to exist for such patients absent approval of toripalimab for patients with platinum-treated NPC. Additionally, despite FDA approval, there will be a continuing need for such therapy in patients who do not receive toripalimab in the first-line setting for a variety of reasons, including challenges with reimbursement or access to newly approved drugs.
L1168: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1169: Based on the review of clinical data from JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02, the clinical and statistical review teams conclude that the Applicant provided substantial evidence of effectiveness for toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adult
L1170: patients with metastatic or with recurrent, locally advanced NPC and for toripalimab as a single agent, in adult patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy.
L1171: JUPITER-02 demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in BIRC-assessed PFS favoring the toripalimab arm, with a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.52 (95% CI 0.36, 0.74; p=0.0003), corresponding to a median PFS of 11.7 months (95% CI: 11, NE) in the toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine arm and 8.0 months (95% CI: 7, 9.5) in the placebo in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine arm. This 3.7-month improvement in median PFS is considered clinically meaningful in this disease setting. These results, supported by a statistically significant improvement in ORR, along with a longer median DOR in the toripalimab plus chemotherapy arm vs the placebo plus chemotherapy arm and no evidence of a detrimental effect on OS, provide substantial evidence of effectiveness for toripalimab, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or with recurrent, locally advanced nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC).
L1172: Among 172 patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing chemotherapy treated with toripalimab as a single agent in POLARIS-02, ORR was 21% (95% CI: 15, 28) with a median duration of response (DOR) of 14.9 months (95% CI: 10.3, NE). Among the 36 patients with an objective response, 83% had a DOR \ge 6 months and 39% had a DOR \ge 12 months. These results are considered clinically meaningful when considering the intended patient population. Furthermore, the results of JUPITER-02 provide further confidence in the benefit of single agent toripalimab in advanced NPC.
L1173: A major limitation of the data is that the two primary trials supporting this marketing application were conducted exclusively in Asia. JUPITER-02 had clinical sites only in China, Taiwan and Singapore, while POLARIS-02 was conducted exclusively in China, and all patients enrolled in both studies were Asian patients. For discussion of this issue, see Section 1 and Section 9.2 of this review.
L1174: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1175: ## Data:
L1176: The safety of toripalimab in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy was evaluated in the following two populations:
L1177: - Patients with GC, ESCC, NPC or HNSCC who received toripalimab 240 mg or 360 mg IV Q3W in combination with standard chemotherapy in Cohorts 5-8 of POLARIS-02 (N=60).
- Patients with NPC who received toripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W in combination with gemcitabine/cisplatin in JUPITER-02 (N=146).
L1178: The safety of toripalimab monotherapy was evaluated in the following 3 populations:
L1179: - Patients with NPC who received toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 (N = 190)
- Patients with a variety of tumor types who have received toripalimab monotherapy at any dose (N = 1066).
L1180: The safety dataset of toripalimab monotherapy includes 11 trials that have completed their primary analysis and two ongoing trials being conducted in the US (TAB001-01) and China (CT8). Only data from the toripalimab monotherapy arms of POLARIS-02 (Cohorts 1-4) and from the toripalimab arm of the randomized trial CT8 are included in the safety dataset of toripalimab monotherapy.
L1181: Safety analyses included examination of common adverse events, deaths, adverse events leading to permanent discontinuation, serious adverse events, and adverse events leading to dose modification. Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) were also evaluated (see Immune-related Adverse Events Adjudication Process). Adverse events were examined, in the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset (N = 1066) by age and gender, allowing an examination of adverse events in a large dataset. While the majority of the patients were Asian, TAB001-01 recruited patients from outside of Asia and adverse events were examined by region. This allowed an examination of both race and of potential differences in the reporting of adverse events by region.
L1182: The on-treatment safety monitoring periods were from the time of first administration of toripalimab to 14-90 days after the last dose of study drug. CT5 and CT15 recorded adverse events up to 60 days after the last dose of toripalimab.
L1183: No pediatric safety data is available for toripalimab.
L1184: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1185: The safety dataset provides an examination of the safety of toripalimab in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy in 146 patients with NPC on JUPITER-02 and 60 patients in Cohorts 5-8 of CT5, and toripalimab monotherapy in 190 patients with NPC. A large safety dataset of 1066 patients with a variety of tumor types is available for the examination of less common adverse events (e.g., irAEs) with toripalimab monotherapy.
L1186: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1187: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the safety results presented by the Applicant. . FDA's review of the safety profile of toripalimab 240 mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine as first-line treatment for locally recurrent or metastatic NPC was based on data from
L1188: 146 patients treated with toripalimab 240 mg Q3W in JUPITER-02. The primary safety analysis populations for FDA's review of the safety profile of toripalimab as a single agent were 190 patients with previously treated NPC in Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02 who received toripalimab 3 mg/kg and a pooled safety dataset of 851 patients with various advanced solid tumors who received toripalimab as a single agent at a dose of 3 mg/kg Q2W across multiple clinical trials. The pooled safety dataset reviewed by the FDA represents a subset of patients included in the Applicant's pooled dataset of 1,066 patients who received toripalimab monotherapy at various doses.
L1189: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1190: ## **Data:**
L1191: Safety analyses included examination of common adverse events, deaths, adverse events leading to permanent discontinuation, adverse events leading to dose modification and serious adverse events in patients who received toripalimab monotherapy or in combination with chemotherapy. The primary safety concern for all drugs in this class is the development of irAEs. Immune-related adverse events were defined as treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs): 1) For which the mechanism of action appeared to be immune-mediated, 2) Which required the use of systemic or topical corticosteroids, other immunosuppressants, or endocrine replacement therapy, and 3) Which had no clear alternative etiology. A pre-defined set of MedDRA preferred terms was used to screen for irAEs. Events which were considered definitely unrelated to study drug were removed. Programmatic and manual approaches were used to link the use of systemic or topical corticosteroids, other immunosuppressants, or endocrine replacement therapy with the adverse events. Grade 1-2 suspected irAEs that were not treated with topical or systemic corticosteroids were excluded. Grade 1-2 endocrine and Grade 1-2 interstitial lung disease were considered irAEs regardless of the use of corticosteroid or hormone replacement therapy. In the final determination, the suspected irAEs were adjudicated by two physicians employed by the Applicant and a second assessment was conducted by an additional Applicant employee. See Immune-related Adverse Events Adjudication Process for a further explanation of the derivation of irAEs.
L1192: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1193: The safety of toripalimab administered either as monotherapy or in combination with chemotherapy in patients with NPC has been adequately studied and characterized.
L1194: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1195: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Throughout the review of safety data, FDA sent multiple information requests (IRs) soliciting additional clinical information about medical causes of death when the patient narratives were unclear or did not provide sufficient information. The FDA sent IRs requesting submission of a revised dataset of laboratory results for the pooled dataset due to a corrupt file included in the initial submission. The FDA also sent an IR soliciting clarification
L1196: regarding events classified as pneumonitis based on preferred terms (PTs) “pneumonia” or “lung infection”.
