L1233: FDA agrees that regarding male predominance, this is consistent with what is expected in the US patient population with NPC. However, the median age of patients in JUPITER-02 (48 years) and Cohort 3 of POLARIS-02 (45 years) is younger than the median age for US patients with NPC (53 or 54 years). Furthermore, given that all patients in the efficacy populations for both studies were of Chinese ethnicity, the efficacy populations do not reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population with NPC, and patients with keratinizing histology are underrepresented in these studies relative to the incidence of this histology in the US patient population with NPC.
L1234: Despite the differences noted above, FDA agrees that the safety data from JUPITER-02, POLARIS-02, and the pooled dataset provide sufficient data for evaluations of the safety profile of toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine and as a single agent.
L1235: ## Adequacy of the safety database
L1236: ## Data:
L1237: The safety dataset provides an examination of the safety of toripalimab in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy in 146 patients with NPC and 60 patients with gastric cancer, squamous cell cancer of the esophagus, NPC, and head and neck cancer. Data is also available for the use of toripalimab monotherapy in 190 patients with NPC and 1066 patients with a variety of tumor types in the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset. Treatment-emergent AEs were defined as AEs that occurred from first administration of toripalimab to 14-90 days after the last dose of study drug. The key trials, POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02, collected adverse events up to 60 days after the last dose of toripalimab. Trials were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice and datasets were carefully assembled.
L1238: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1239: The size and quality of the safety dataset is adequate to define the risks of toripalimab administered as monotherapy or in combination with chemotherapy.
L1240: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1241: FDA did not perform analyses on data from the 60 patients from POLARIS-02 Cohorts 5-8 because these patients received chemotherapy regimens other than cisplatin and gemcitabine and received toripalimab at various doses. FDA's review of the safety of toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine focused on the safety data from the experimental arm of JUPITER-02 (N=146).
L1242: FDA did not perform analyses on the pooled monotherapy dataset of 1,066 patients because these patients received toripalimab at doses ranging from 0.3 mg/kg Q2W to 10 mg/kg Q2W and from 80 mg Q2W to 480 mg Q2W. Instead, FDA focused the safety analysis of toripalimab monotherapy on a subset of the pooled dataset comprising 851 patients treated with toripalimab 3 mg/kg Q2W.
L1243: FDA agrees that the size and quality of the safety data allows for an adequate assessment of the safety profile of toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine and as a single agent.
L1244: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1245: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1246: ## Data:
L1247: Clinical trials were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice. POLARIS-02 was monitored by IQVIA and JUPITER-02 by Parexel International and provide suitable data for review in accordance with 21 CFR 312.120 and 314.106.
L1248: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1249: No issues were identified regarding data integrity or submission quality that had an impact on the clinical safety review. The submission quality was sufficient for a thorough review of the safety of toripalimab and included adequate narratives in the categories agreed to by the FDA.
L1250: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1251: FDA agrees that the quality of the submitted data was generally sufficient for a review of the safety of toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine and as a single agent. However, FDA identified potential deficiencies in data quality requiring data review procedures or corrective measures from the Applicant, including a corrupt file of laboratory results from the pooled safety datasets and narratives containing scarce clinical information regarding potential causes of death for patients who died within 30 days of the last study dose. These deficiencies were able to be adequately addressed through information requests.
L1252: ## **Categorization of Adverse Event**
L1253: ## **Data:**
L1254: The following versions of MedDRA and the NCI-CTCAE have been used in analyzing the safety database.
L1255: - Data from JUPITER-02, including irAEs, was mapped using MedDRA v23.0 and graded using CTCAE v5.0.
- Data from POLARIS-02 as reported in the CSR, was mapped using MedDRA v23.0 and graded using CTCAE v4.03.
- Immune-related adverse events from all studies other than JUPITER-02 (including POLARIS-02) were mapped using MedDRA v21.0 and graded with CTCAE v4.03.
- Analyses for the toripalimab monotherapy Safety Dataset used MedDRA v21.0 and CTCAE v4.03.
L1256: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1257: Adverse events have been categorized using widely accepted methods such as MedDRA and the CTCAE.
L1258: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1259: FDA agrees with Applicant's statement.
L1260: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1261: ## Data:
L1262: Routine laboratory tests included hematology, chemistries, thyroid function, and urinalysis. Immunogenicity and 12-lead ECG were assessed in each study. The key trials, POLARIS-02 and JUPITER-02, collected hematology, chemistries, and urinalysis every 2 weeks or 3 weeks respectively, and thyroid function tests each cycle.
L1263: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1264: Sufficient testing has been done to ensure a thorough understanding of the safety of toripalimab.
L1265: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1266: FDA agrees with Applicant's statement.
L1267: ## 8.2.4. * Safety Results
L1268: ## Deaths
L1269: ## Data:
L1270: The table below provides information on the patients in JUPITER-02 and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 whose deaths occurred within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab. In JUPITER-02, there was 1 death in the toripalimab arm and 2 deaths in the placebo arm within 30 days of the last dose. Adverse events accounted for 2 of the deaths (1 in each arm) while 1 (placebo arm) was due to disease progression. In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, 9 (4.7%) deaths occurred within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab. Adverse events were the most common cause of death accounting for 6 of 9 deaths. The remaining deaths were due to disease progression (N=2) and Other/No Cause Recorded (1).

L1280: In Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02, there was 1 death within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab. This was a death due to suicide in a patient in the ESCC cohort.
L1281: In the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset, 74 (6.9%) patients died within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab. This includes 46 deaths due to an adverse event, 24 deaths due to disease progression, and 4 due to Other/No Cause Recorded. The 46 deaths due to an adverse event included death NOS (N=8), respiratory failure (6), disease progression (reported as an AE) (5), lung infection (5), and multi-organ dysfunction syndrome (3). The remaining AEs leading to death occurred in \le 2 patients.
L1282: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1283: The incidence of deaths on JUPITER-02 (first-line treatment) was lower than Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 (later-line treatment). The incidence of patient deaths is consistent with that of other drugs in this class. In a small number of patients, a cause of death was not recorded and death NOS was reported as an AE. Narratives are available for all patients and include all AEs that occurred within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab.
L1284: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1285: In JUPITER-02, while only one patient death due to AE within 30 days of last dose of toripalimab was reported, the narratives indicated that three patients in the toripalimab arm experienced a fatal AE. One patient died of epistaxis within 30 days of the last dose of toripalimab. One patient died 60 days after the last dose due to an intracranial hemorrhage in the context of multiple immune-related AEs, including myositis, hepatitis, thrombocytopenia, and myocarditis. One patient died 39 days after the last dose from pneumonia.
L1286: In POLARIS-02/Cohort 3, the Applicant classified one death as “Not due to an AE” and subclassified the event as “Other/No Cause Recorded”. The corresponding narrative indicates that the patient developed a Grade 2 increase in alanine aminotransferase on the date of the last dose of toripalimab and died 29 days later. The narrative stated that “disease progression could not be excluded.” FDA sent an IR to investigate the possibility of immune-mediated hepatitis. In response
L1287: to the IR, the Applicant stated that no additional clinical information was available to further elucidate the cause of death.
L1288: Of the 6 deaths due to AEs occurring within 30 days of last study dose for POLARIS-02/Cohort 3, three were reported as “death NOS”. One of the “death NOS” events involved a 45 year-old male patient who developed recurrent abdominal pain and distention in the context of use of fentanyl transdermal patches. Imaging studies of unclear modality revealed “small, scattered liver metastases” which were unlikely to explain the abdominal symptoms. The patient died 24 days after the last study dose without other diagnostic evaluations reported in the narrative. The second “death NOS” event involved a 38 year-old male patient who died at home 7 days after the last study dose. The last laboratory evaluation 8 days before death revealed multiple electrolyte abnormalities, including hyponatremia of 125. The narrative states that no further workup took place for the electrolyte abnormalities or for the disease status. The third event of “Death NOS” refers to a 52 year-old male patient who likely died of recurrent and major episodes of epistaxis. The remaining 3 deaths occurring within 30 days of last dose of toripalimab were due to Grade 4 hyponatremia, sudden death, and hyperbilirubinemia from hepatic failure, in the context of baseline chronic Hepatitis B, Grade 2 hepatic insufficiency, and hepatic metastases. In response to an IR, the Applicant stated that no additional clinical information was available to allow further determinations of the causes of death for these 3 patients.
L1289: Narratives for two additional deaths in patients who experienced SAEs were reviewed. A 36 year-old male patient developed progressive ascites and anasarca, starting 12 days after the first and last study dose. The patient died 39 days after the last study dose. The Applicant attributed the ascites and anasarca to tumor progression, but no reported imaging or other studies confirm disease progression in the narrative.
L1290: A 42 year-old female patient died 56 days after the last study dose. During treatment with toripalimab, the patient developed dermatomyositis and started a course of oral prednisone 19 days before the last study dose. The patient also developed a peri-auricular subcutaneous abscess requiring hospitalization 13 days after the last study dose. The patient underwent drainage of the abscess and a course of intravenous antibiotics. The abscess was reported as “ongoing” at the time of discharge, 21 days after the last study dose. On the day of death, the patient developed fever, dyspnea, and diffuse pain prompting hospitalization. The Applicant considered the cause of death as unknown and possibly related to lung infection. In response to an IR, the applicant stated that no additional clinical information was available to further elucidate the cause of death for this patient.
L1291: The overall incidences of death due to AEs were 2.1%, 4.7%, and 7% in the JUPITER-02 trial, the POLARIS-02/Cohort 3 trial, and the pooled safety dataset, respectively.
L1292: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1293: ## Data:
L1294: Serious adverse events (SAEs) that occurred in at least 2% of patients on JUPITER-02, Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02, and Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 are included in the tables below. Laboratory abnormalities reported as SAEs were not included in the tables.
L1295: The incidence of SAEs, 41.4% in the toripalimab and 43.4% in the placebo arms of JUPITER-02, was very similar. While the incidence of SAEs differs between the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02 and Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02, only 12/60 patients from Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02 had NPC. Among patients receiving toripalimab monotherapy, 24.2% of patients on Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 and 27.8% in the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset (N = 1066) experienced a SAE. In both Cohort 3 and the monotherapy safety dataset, lung infection or pneumonia was the most common SAE followed by abnormal hepatic function.

L1302: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1303: The incidence of SAEs is consistent with other drugs in this class or with the types of events that are typically seen in patients with advanced tumors.
L1304: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1305: When reviewing SAEs, FDA's process for grouping preferred terms resulted in slightly different percentages. In addition, the FDA included laboratory abnormalities in the review of SAEs.
L1306: In JUPITER-02, 43% of patients had one or more SAEs. Aside from the laboratory abnormalities resulting in SAEs, FDA's review was generally similar to the Applicant's review. The exception was hepatic function abnormal, in which FDA's review showed an incidence of 2.7% versus 1.4% reported by the Applicant. FDA notes that the Group Term Hepatic function abnormal used in FDA's analysis included the Preferred Terms "hepatic function abnormal" and "liver function test abnormal."
L1307: In POLARIS-02/Cohort 3, 24% of patients had one or more SAEs. The most frequent SAEs (\ge 2\%) included pneumonia, hepatic function abnormal, and hyperbilirubinemia. The FDA estimates of SAEs aligned with Applicant's estimates. In the pooled safety dataset, 23% of patients had one or more SAEs.

L1319: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1320: ## Data:
L1321: The most common AEs (&gt; 1%) leading to permanent discontinuation of toripalimab are shown in the table below. Laboratory abnormalities that resulted in permanent discontinuation were not included. In JUPITER-02, the most common causes of permanent discontinuation were lung infection or pneumonia followed by pulmonary tuberculosis and rash. Permanent discontinuation due to tuberculosis in JUPITER-02 occurred in both arms. One patient in the toripalimab arm had a history of tuberculosis and developed disease reactivation in the absence of steroids. There were 3 additional patients in the toripalimab arm who had a history of tuberculosis and did not develop reactivation. The other patient in the toripalimab arm received the BCG vaccine prior to the report of tuberculosis and had not received corticosteroids. The patient in the placebo arm did not have a history of tuberculosis and had not received systemic steroids. In the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset, 12.5% of patients had an adverse event that resulted in permanent discontinuation. The most common AE leading to permanent discontinuation in this dataset was disease progression.

L1327: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1328: The incidence of AEs leading to permanent discontinuation is consistent with other drugs in this class or the types of events typically seen in patients with advanced tumors. The number of patients who discontinued due to pulmonary tuberculosis needs to be considered in the context of the incidence of tuberculosis in China and in the context of the 3 patients with a history of tuberculosis who did not develop reactivation.
L1329: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1330: In JUPITER-02, 12% of patients discontinued toripalimab due to AEs. AEs leading to toripalimab discontinuation in \ge 1\% of patients were pneumonia (2.1%), pulmonary tuberculosis (1.4%), rash (1.4%), vomiting (1.4%), and platelet count decreased (1.4%).
L1331: In POLARIS-02/Cohort 3, 9% of patients discontinued toripalimab due to AEs. The AEs leading to toripalimab discontinuation in \ge 1\% of patients included pneumonia (1.1%), hepatic function abnormal (1.1%), and hyperbilirubinemia (1.1%). The FDA estimates of toripalimab discontinuation due to AEs generally aligned with Applicant's estimates; the exception was hyperbilirubinemia, which the Applicant did not report.
L1332: In the pooled safety dataset, 11% of patients discontinued toripalimab due to AEs. AEs leading to toripalimab discontinuation in \ge 1\% of patients included pneumonitis (1.4%).
L1333: ## **Dose Interruption Due to Adverse Effects**
L1334: ## **Data:**
L1335: Toripalimab could be interrupted, but the dose could not be reduced. The table below provides information on the AEs leading to interruption of toripalimab. Laboratory abnormalities that resulted in interruption were not included in the table. In both JUPITER-02 and in Cohorts 5-
L1336: 8/POLARIS-02, when toripalimab was combined with chemotherapy, laboratory abnormalities led to numerous interruptions.
L1337: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 (N=190), 22.6% of patients interrupted toripalimab monotherapy due to an AE. The most common cause of dose interruption (&gt;2%) was lung infection (2.1%). In the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset, 19.7% of patients interrupted toripalimab. The most common adverse event leading to interruption was again lung infection.

L1359: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1360: Data:
L1361: The most significant AEs in patients treated with PD-1/L1 inhibitors are immune-related adverse events. These AEs are due to the underlying mechanism of action of the drug, activation of the immune system. The table below provides information on the incidence of all grade and \ge Grade 3 irAEs in patients who received: 1) toripalimab monotherapy (Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 and 12 other trials), 2) toripalimab in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy (JUPITER-02 and Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02), and 3) all treated patients. This is derived from the sponsor-adjudicated irAEs (See Immune-related Adverse Events Adjudication Process). Overall, 26.7% of patients experienced an irAE with 5.3% of patients experiencing a \ge Grade 3 adverse event. The number of patients who experienced an irAE while receiving toripalimab monotherapy, 26.1%, is very similar to the 29.6% incidence of irAEs when toripalimab was used in combination with gemcitabine/cisplatin. Thirty-nine patients (3.1%) developed at least one irAEs leading to permanent discontinuation. The most common irAEs resulting in discontinuation were immune-related hepatitis (0.9%) and immune-related pneumonitis (0.9%). Further information on irAEs is provided below.

L1366: ## Immune-related Pneumonitis
L1367: Immune-related pneumonitis was reported in 28/1272 (2.2%) of patients treated with toripalimab including 2 (0.2%) Grade 5, 7 (0.6%) Grade 3, and 11 (0.9%) Grade 2 events. Toripalimab was permanently discontinued in 11/1272 (0.9%). Of the 6/1272 (0.5%) patients in whom toripalimab was temporarily interrupted due to pneumonitis, 2 resumed toripalimab and neither experienced recurrent pneumonitis. Corticosteroids were administered to 21/28 (75.0%) patients. Pneumonitis resolved in 8/28 (28.6%) of patients. Brief narratives for the 2 deaths due to pneumonitis are provided below.
L1368: - Patient CT3- (b) (6) was a 54 year old Asian male with Stage IV squamous, non-small cell lung cancer whose CT scan on Study Day 148 showed a large new area of opacity in the upper and middle lobes of the right lung (new lesion). Differential diagnosis included pneumonitis and disease progression. Infectious causes were ruled out and the patient underwent needle biopsy of the lung on Day 159 which confirmed the diagnosis of pneumonitis (Grade 2). Toripalimab was permanently discontinued and the patient initiated prednisone 60 mg per day on Day 177. He had persistent shortness of breath on Day 187. On Day 202, he developed an acute exacerbation of dyspnea at home and died later that day. An autopsy was not performed.
- Patient CT5 (Cohort 1) (b) (6) was a 66 year old Asian female with stage IV gastric adenocarcinoma. The patient was admitted to a local hospital 16 days after receiving the first dose of toripalimab (patient received 2 doses of toripalimab) with fever (maximum 39.2°C) and was treated for community-acquired pneumonia with antibacterial and antifungal antibiotics. A CT scan showed “thickened and cord-like interlobular septa in both lungs” and low-density shadows in the right lobe of liver with unclear borders. A diagnosis of interstitial lung disease was made, toripalimab was permanently discontinued, and approximately 9 days after admission, the patient initiated dexamethasone 40 mg per day with an attempted taper starting approximately 10 days later. The patient died on Study Day 46; an autopsy was not performed. The investigator-assessed the primary cause of death was a combination of clinical disease progression, interstitial pneumonitis, and hepatitis.
L1369: ## Immune-related Colitis
L1370: Immune-related colitis was reported in 3/1272 (0.2%) patients receiving toripalimab, including 2 (0.2%) Grade 3 and 1 (0.1%) Grade 2 events. No patients required permanent discontinuations. Toripalimab was temporarily interrupted in 1/1272 (0.1%) patient due to colitis and was reinitiated after symptom improvement. No patients received corticosteroids due to colitis. Colitis resolved in all patients.
L1371: (b) (4)
L1372: (toripalimab-tpzi)
L1373: ## Immune-related Hepatitis
L1374: Immune-related hepatitis was reported in 34/1272 (2.7%) patients treated with toripalimab including 7 (0.6%) Grade 4, 22 (1.7%) Grade 3, and 5 (0.4%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 11/1272 (1.0%) and temporary interruption in 10/1272 (0.8%) patients. Five were able to reinitiate toripalimab without exacerbation of their abnormality. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 10/34 (29.4%) of patients. Hepatitis resolved in 16/34 (47.1%) of patients. One patient had myocarditis (discussed below) along with an increase in liver enzymes. No patients met Hy's law criteria.
L1375: ## Immune-related Endocrine Disorders
L1376: ## *Thyroid Disease*
L1377: Hypothyroidism was reported in 194/1272 (15.3%), hyperthyroidism in 73 (5.7%), and thyroiditis in 8 (0.6%) patients treated with toripalimab. There were no reports of Grade 3-5 hypothyroidism or thyroiditis. Grade 3 hyperthyroidism occurred in 1 (0.1%) patient. Grade 2 events included hypothyroidism in 110 (8.6%), hyperthyroidism in 18 (1.4%), and thyroiditis in 3 (0.2%) patients.
L1378: Permanent discontinuation occurred in 1/1272 (0.1%) patients with hypothyroidism and no patients with hyperthyroidism or thyroiditis. Toripalimab was temporarily interrupted in 8/1272 (0.6%) patients due to hypothyroidism, 3 (0.2%) with hyperthyroidism, and none with thyroiditis. Six of the 8 patients who interrupted toripalimab due to hypothyroidism and all 3 patients who interrupted due to hyperthyroidism were able to resume study drug. None of the patients received corticosteroids for hypothyroidism. One patient received corticosteroids for hyperthyroidism and 2 for thyroiditis. Thyroiditis resolved in 1 patient. One patient (CT4 (b) (6)) with hyperthyroidism, hypothyroidism, and thyroiditis also reported adrenocortical hypofunction.
L1379: ## *Adrenal Insufficiency*
L1380: Adrenal insufficiency was reported in 6/1272 (0.5%) patients receiving toripalimab. No patient had a Grade 3-5 event and 5 (0.4%) had a Grade 2 event. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 1 (0.1%) and temporary interruption in 1 (0.1%) patient. Corticosteroids were administered to 5/6 (83.3%) patients. One patient who interrupted toripalimab was able to resume treatment after symptom improvement.
L1381: ## *Diabetes Mellitus*
L1382: Diabetes mellitus was reported in 11/1272 (0.9%) patients receiving toripalimab including 2 (0.2%) Grade 4, 8 (0.6%) Grade 3 and 1 (0.1%) Grade 2 event. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 3/1272 (0.2%) and temporary interruption in 1/1272 (0.1%) patients. No patients received corticosteroids and 7/11 (63.6%) patients were treated with insulin. The majority of patients had hyperglycemia or a prior diagnosis of diabetes at baseline. One patient (CT5 (b) (6)) who had a
L1383: normal baseline glucose level developed new onset diabetes on Day 346. The onset of diabetes was associated with an infection and the patient required IV insulin and hydration. Anti-islet cell and anti-insulin antibodies were negative.
L1384: ## Hypophysitis
L1385: Hypophysitis occurred in 3/1272 (0.2%) patients receiving toripalimab including 2 (0.2%) Grade 3 and 1 (0.1%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 1/1272 (0.1%) and temporary interruption in 1 (0.1%) patient. All patients required treatment with corticosteroids. One patient who interrupted toripalimab was able to resume treatment but developed Grade 1 pneumonitis.
L1386: ## Immune-related Nephritis
L1387: Immune-mediated nephritis occurred in 5 (0.4%) patients receiving toripalimab including 4 (0.3%) Grade 3 and 1 (0.1%) Grade 2 events. There were no permanent discontinuations or temporary interruptions for immune-related nephritis and none of the patients required corticosteroids. Nephritis resolved in 3/5 (60.0%) patients.
L1388: ## Immune-related Skin Disorders
L1389: Dermatological toxicity was reported in 54/1272 (4.2%) patients receiving toripalimab including 9 (0.7%) Grade 3 and 16 (1.3%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 4/1272 (0.3%) and temporary interruption in 3 (0.2%) patients. All 3 patients who interrupted toripalimab were able to resume treatment. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 10/54 (18.5%) patients. Topical corticosteroids were administered to 41/54 (75.9%) patients. Immune-mediated dermatological toxicity resolved in 34/54 (63.0%) patients. There were no reports of Stevens-Johnson syndrome or toxic epidermal necrolysis.
L1390: ## Immune-related Myocarditis
L1391: Myocarditis was reported in 5/1272 (0.4%) patients including 1 (0.1%) Grade 4, 1 (0.1%) Grade 3, and 3 (0.2%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 4/1272 (0.3%) patients due to myocarditis and 1 additional patient due to myositis. No patients temporarily interrupted toripalimab. Corticosteroids were administered to all patients and all discontinued treatment. Two patients (CT12- (b) (6), CT15- (b) (6)) had both myocarditis and myositis.
L1392: ## Immune-related Myositis
L1393: Immune-mediated myositis occurred in 5 (0.4%) patients including 1 (0.1%) Grade 4, 3 (0.2%) Grade 3, and 1 (0.1%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 4/1272 (0.3%) patients and 1 (0.1%) patient interrupted toripalimab. All patients received corticosteroids.
L1394: ## Other irAEs
L1395: ## *Immune-related Pancreatitis*
L1396: Immune-related pancreatitis was reported in 6/1272 (0.5%) patients receiving toripalimab, including 1 (0.1%) Grade 4 and 4 (0.3%) Grade 2 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 4/1272 (0.3%) patients and temporary interruption in 1 (0.1%) patient. One patient received corticosteroids for treatment of immune-related pancreatitis.
L1397: ## *Immune-related Ocular Toxicity*
L1398: On Day 70, 1/1272 (0.1%) patient developed Grade 1 iritis (CT4/ (b) (6)) and was treated with corticosteroid eye drops. He initially remained on toripalimab but then permanently discontinued due to disease progression and iritis. He began vemurafenib. Decreased vision was reported 28 days after the last dose and Grade 3 uveitis and cataracts were diagnosed 43 days after the last dose of toripalimab. Vemurafenib was held. He was treated with oral corticosteroids and corticosteroid eye drops. The patient improved and was able to resume vemurafenib at a reduced dose but remained on corticosteroid eye drops.
L1399: ## *Immune-related Thrombocytopenia*
L1400: Immune-related thrombocytopenia was reported in 5/1272 (0.4%) patients receiving toripalimab including 1 (0.1%) Grade 5, 3 (0.2%) Grade 4, and 1 (0.1%) Grade 3 events. Permanent discontinuation occurred in 3/1272 (0.2%) patients and temporary interruption in 2 (0.2%) patients. Corticosteroids were administered to 2/5 (40.0%) patients. A brief narrative for the 1 death due to thrombocytopenia is provided below.
L1401: - Patient (b) (6): A 45 year-old Chinese male developed myositis 7 days after his last dose of toripalimab followed by the development of myocarditis, thrombocytopenia, and renal insufficiency. He was treated with corticosteroids, infliximab, and mycophenolate mofetil as well as plasma infusions, fibrinogen, thrombopoietin, and platelet transfusion. He responded to the platelet transfusion with an increase in platelets from 2 to 33 x 109/L. This, however, was followed by loss of consciousness. CT showed intraventricular hemorrhage and obstructive hydrocephalus. On the same day, he was found to have gram negative bacteremia. He died later that day.
L1402: ## *Non-infectious Cystitis*
L1403: One patient (b) (6) developed Grade 1 non-infectious cystitis. This did not result in permanent discontinuation or drug interruption and the patient did not receive steroids.
L1404: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1405: The development of irAEs was expected based on the mechanism of action of the drug. The pattern, incidence and severity of irAEs was consistent with other drugs in this class and was manageable with the use of appropriate dose interruption and immunosuppressants.
L1406: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1407: FDA reviewed the Applicant adjudicated immune-related AEs (irAEs) in JUPITER-02 and in the pooled safety dataset. The Applicant did not provide a separate dataset of adjudicated irAEs for POLARIS-02/Cohort 3.

L1411: One patient in JUPITER-02 had a fatal AE of intracranial hemorrhage associated with immune-related thrombocytopenia. This patient was a 45 year old male who developed multiple immune-related AEs, sepsis with gram-negative rod bacteremia, and coagulopathy. Two patients in the pooled dataset had fatal immune-related pneumonitis.

L1413: (toripalimab-tpzi)
L1414: During review, FDA noted that in JUPITER-02, 2 (1.4%) patients did not have toripalimab withheld or receive systemic corticosteroids for the management of grade 2 pneumonitis, when the protocol required that these actions be taken. In the pooled safety dataset, 29 (3.4%) patients did not have toripalimab withheld or did not receive systemic corticosteroids for the management of grade 3 or 4 immune-related hepatitis, nephritis colitis, uveitis, and platelet count decreased.
L1415: Additional results of FDA analyses and review used to inform Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) of proposed labeling are presented below.
L1416: ## *Pneumonitis*
L1417: JUPITER-02: Immune-mediated pneumonitis occurred in 2.1% (3/146) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 2 (1.4%) adverse reactions. Pneumonitis resolved in 67% (2/3) of these patients.
L1418: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Immune-mediated pneumonitis occurred in 2.6% (22/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including fatal (0.2%), Grade 3 (0.7%), and Grade 2 (1.1%) adverse reactions; there were no cases of Grade 4 pneumonitis. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 82% (18/22) of patients with pneumonitis. Pneumonitis led to permanent discontinuation of (b) (4) in 1.2% (10/851) of patients. Pneumonitis resolved in 23% (5/22) of these patients.
L1419: ## *Colitis*
L1420: JUPITER-02: No patients developed immune-mediated colitis.
L1421: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Immune-mediated colitis occurred in 0.4% (3/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 3 (0.2%) and Grade 2 (0.1%) adverse reactions. Colitis resolved in all 3 patients.
L1422: ## *Hepatotoxicity and Immune-Mediated Hepatitis*
L1423: JUPITER-02: Immune-mediated hepatitis occurred in 0.7% (1/146) of patients receiving toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, which was a Grade 3 (0.7%) adverse reaction. The patient with immune-mediated hepatitis required systemic corticosteroids.
L1424: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Immune-mediated hepatitis occurred in 3.3% (28/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 4 (0.8%), Grade 3 (2.1%), and Grade 2 (0.4%) adverse reactions. Hepatitis led to permanent discontinuation of (b) (4) in 1.1% of patients and withholding of toripalimab in 0.8% of patients. Hepatitis resolved in 54% (15/28) of these patients.
L1425: ## *Adrenal Insufficiency*
L1426: JUPITER-02: No patients developed adrenal insufficiency.
L1427: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Adrenal insufficiency occurred in 0.5% (4/851) of the patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 2 (0.4%) and Grade 1 (0.1%) adverse reactions. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 75% (3/4) of the patients with adrenal insufficiency. Adrenal insufficiency led to withholding of toripalimab in 0.1% (1/851) of patients. In the one patient in whom toripalimab was withheld, toripalimab was reinitiated after symptom improvement.
L1428: ## *Hypophysitis*
L1429: JUPITER-02: No patients developed hypophysitis.
L1430: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Hypophysitis occurred in 0.4% (3/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 3 (0.2%) and Grade 2 (0.1%) adverse reactions. All three patients received systemic corticosteroids. Hypophysitis led to permanent discontinuation of toripalimab in 0.1% (1/851) of patients and withholding of toripalimab in 0.1% (1/851) of patients. The one patient in whom toripalimab was withheld reinitiated toripalimab.
L1431: ## *Thyroid Disorders*
L1432: JUPITER-02: Thyroiditis occurred in 2.1% (3/146) of patients receiving toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, including Grade 2 (1.4%). Three patients required thyroid hormone replacement therapy. Thyroiditis resolved in one of the 3 patients.
L1433: Hyperthyroidism occurred in 1.4% (2/146) of patients receiving toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine. Hyperthyroidism resolved in these 2 patients.
L1434: Hypothyroidism occurred in 30% (44/146) of patients receiving toripalimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, including Grade 2 (24%) and Grade 1 (6%). Eighty percent of the 44 patients required thyroid hormone replacement therapy. Toripalimab was withheld in 2.1% (3/146) of the patients. Of the 3 patients in whom toripalimab was withheld, 2 patients reinitiated toripalimab.
L1435: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Thyroiditis occurred in 0.6% (5/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 2 (0.1%). Two of these 5 patients received systemic corticosteroids and 2 required thyroid hormone replacement therapy. Thyroiditis resolved in 2 of the 5 patients.
L1436: Hyperthyroidism occurred in 67% (545/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 2 (1.98%). Hyperthyroidism resolved in 85% (467/545) of the patients.
L1437: Hypothyroidism occurred in 15% (128/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 2 (8%). Sixty three percent of the 128 patients required thyroid hormone replacement therapy. Toripalimab was withheld in 0.5% of patients. Of the 4 patients in whom toripalimab was withheld, 3 patients reinitiated toripalimab.
L1438: ## *Type 1 Diabetes Mellitus*
L1439: JUPITER-02: No patients developed diabetes mellitus.
L1440: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Diabetes mellitus occurred in 0.9% (8/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 4 (0.1%), Grade 3 (0.7%), and Grade 2 (0.1%). Diabetes mellitus led to permanent discontinuation of toripalimab in 0.4% of patients. Six of the 8 (75%) patients with diabetes mellitus required long-term insulin therapy.
L1441: ## *Nephritis with Renal Dysfunction*
L1442: JUPITER-02: Immune-mediated nephritis occurred in 0.7% (1/146) of patients receiving toripalimab. The one patient with immune-mediated nephritis (Grade 4) required systemic corticosteroids and nephritis led to discontinuation of toripalimab. Nephritis resolved in this patient.
L1443: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Immune-mediated nephritis occurred in 0.5% (4/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 3 (0.5%) adverse reactions. Nephritis resolved in 75% (3/4) of these patients.
L1444: ## *Immune-Mediated Dermatologic Adverse Reactions*
L1445: JUPITER-02: Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions occurred in 8% (12/146) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 3 (3.4%) and Grade 2 (1.4%) adverse reactions. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 25% (3/12) of the patients with immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions. Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions led to permanent discontinuation of toripalimab in 2.1% (3) of patients. Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions resolved in 92% (11/12) of these patients.
L1446: Pooled monotherapy dataset: Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions occurred in 4% (34/851) of patients receiving toripalimab, including Grade 3 (0.4%) and Grade 2 (1.4%) adverse reactions. Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions led to withholding of toripalimab in 0.4% (3) of the patients. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 12% (4/34) of the patients with immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions. Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions resolved in 71% (24/34) of these patients.
L1447: ## **Infusion-related Reactions**
L1448: Among all patients treated with toripalimab, 33/1272 (2.6%) patients developed an infusion-related reaction (IRR). The incidence was 2.1% (22/1066) with toripalimab monotherapy and 5.3% (11/206) when toripalimab was combined with chemotherapy. There were no Grade 4-5 events and one Grade 3 event that involved bronchospasm, dyspnea, flushing, hypertension, and tachycardia immediately after initiation of the first dose of toripalimab. The patient was treated with systemic and inhaled steroids along with several bronchodilators. Dyspnea resolved after 2 hours. Prior to her second dose, the patient was pre-treated with diphenhydramine and dexamethasone and did not develop an infusion reaction.
L1449: These 33 patients include seven patients who had an adverse reaction coded as an infusion-related reaction (preferred term) and an additional 26 patients with an AE that was not coded as an infusion-related reaction but where the event was designated by the Investigator as an infusion-related reaction (Question on case report form-Is this an infusion reaction? Y/N).
L1450: Among all patients who received toripalimab (N=1272), 2/109 (1.8%) of those with ADA and 31/1163 (2.7%) of those without experienced an IRR. In the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset (N = 1066), IRRs occurred in 1.0% of patients with ADA and in 2.2% of those without ADA. When toripalimab was combined with chemotherapy, 1/8 (12.5%) patients with ADA and 10/198 (5.1%) without ADA developed an IRR.
L1451: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1452: The development of infusion-related reactions is expected with therapeutic protein products administered intravenously. The incidence was similar to that with other drugs in this class and was manageable with the use of appropriate slowing or interruption of the infusion and standard medical management as needed.
L1453: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1454: FDA generally agrees with Applicant's position. In JUPITER-02, 4.1% of patients experienced an infusion-related reaction (IRR) in the toripalimab arm, which were Grade 2 (0.7%) and Grade 1 (3.5%). In the pooled safety dataset, 2.0% of patients experienced an IRR, which were Grade 3 (0.1%), Grade 2 (0.6%), and Grade 1 (1.3%).

L1478: The number of adverse events was relatively low and was consistent with other drugs in this class. In the patients with NPC, the relatively higher incidence of hypothyroidism may have been due to prior radiation to the neck as well as the use of a PD-1 inhibitor.

L1504: ## Laboratory Findings
L1505: ## Data:

L1507: The table below provides information on worsening laboratory abnormalities that occurred in &gt; 20% of patients on JUPITER-02. Substantial hematological toxicity was seen when toripalimab was combined with cisplatin/gemcitabine. A small number of patients on each study experienced Grade 3-4 increases in ALT and AST. Note that while Grade 3-4 hyponatremia was common in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the incidence of hyponatremia in the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02 was lower than that in the placebo arm. These laboratory abnormalities have been described in other trials of PD-1 inhibitors. No patients met Hy's law criteria.

L1519: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1520: The incidence and severity of laboratory abnormalities seen on JUPITER-02 and POLARIS-02 are consistent with those seen with other drugs in this class and with the use of cytotoxic chemotherapy.
L1521: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1522: In reviewing discrepancies between laboratory abnormalities identified by the Applicant and the FDA, FDA identified a potential error in how the ADBL datasets were developed. For serum calcium and other laboratory parameters, the ADBL dataset for POLARIS-2/Cohort 3 set the upper and lower normal limits to the same value (e.g., 2 mmol/l for calcium). This pattern affects other laboratory parameters including magnesium and may extend to the entire pooled safety dataset of toripalimab monotherapy. The error could have introduced inaccuracies in the evaluations of laboratory abnormalities for POLARIS-02. A comment requesting that this be addressed if the application is resubmitted will be included in the Complete Response letter.
L1523: Laboratory abnormalities occurring in 20% or more of patients regardless of the absolute differences between the placebo and chemotherapy arm, based on FDA's review of the current datasets are presented below. For JUPITER-02, FDA results for all laboratory abnormalities based are on the updated 90-day safety data.

L1532: ## Vital Signs
L1533: ## Data:
L1534: No individual study identified a safety signal related to changes in vital signs. In the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02, the mean pre-dose pulse rates changed from 79.9 \pm 12.15 (N=146) to 87.2 \pm 14.78
L1535: beats per minutes at the end of treatment (N=41). The mean pre-dose systolic blood pressures varied from 118.1 \pm 14.13 mm Hg (N=146) to 118.8 \pm 14.6 (N=41) and mean diastolic pressure from 73.2 \pm 9.4 (N=146) at baseline to 75.9 \pm 9.8 (N=41) at the end of treatment, respectively.
L1536: In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, mean pulse rate changed from 81.3 \pm (13.1) beats per minute (N=190) to 84.0 \pm (12.6) (N=95) at the end of treatment. Mean systolic blood pressure varied from 113.4 \pm (13.3) mm Hg (N=190) to 115.5 \pm (14.8) (N=94) and mean diastolic pressure from 72.1 \pm (9.3) mm Hg (N=190) to 73.5 \pm (9.1) (N = 94) at baseline and the end of treatment, respectively.
L1537: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1538: The effect of toripalimab on the patients' vital signs is not clinically important.
L1539: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1540: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L1541: ## **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)**
L1542: ## **Data:**
L1543: In JUPITER-02, ECGs were obtained at baseline and as clinically indicated. In POLARIS-02, ECGs were obtained at baseline, each cycle, and at the end of treatment visit. ECGs were not obtained in triplicate and thus extreme values might reflect normal physiological variability in measurements rather than true abnormalities. In Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, by ECG, the mean heart rate was 84.4 \pm 14.6 beats per minute at baseline (N=189) and 84.2 \pm 17.8 at the end of treatment (N=97). The mean PR interval was 147.3 \pm 16.4 ms (N=189) and 145.8 \pm 19.4 ms (N=96) at baseline and end of treatment, respectively. Finally, the mean QRS was 95.0 \pm 12.5 ms at baseline (N=189) and 94.6 \pm 13.3 ms at the end of treatment (N=97).
L1544: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1545: There was little change in the heart rate or ECG intervals with toripalimab.
L1546: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1547: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L1548: ## **QT**
L1549: ## **Data:**
L1550: ## ***Toripalimab Monotherapy***
L1551: QT interval data were captured in the case report forms for five studies (CT4, CT5, CT8, CT12 and TAB001-01), in which four studies (CT5, CT8, CT12 and TAB001-01) the QT intervals were corrected
L1552: for heart rate. TAB001-01 performed triplicate ECGs at baseline and during treatment in the dose-escalation phase.
L1553: In the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset (N = 1066), among the 676 patients exposed to toripalimab who had a baseline and at least one post-baseline QT assessment recorded, the mean change from baseline for the QT interval was a decrease of 2.1 ms, 0.9% had a maximum post-treatment QT interval of &gt;500 ms, and 9.8% had a maximum increase in QT interval &gt;60 ms. While the QT interval was recorded in 676 patients, 628 patients exposed to toripalimab had at least one post-treatment corrected QT assessment. The mean change from baseline for QTc interval was an increase of 1.6 ms, 2.9% had a maximum QTc interval of &gt; 500 ms, and 5.4% had a maximum increased in QTc of &gt; 60 ms.
L1554: A total of 89 patients with a post-treatment QTc and/or QT interval of &gt;500 ms or an increase from baseline in QTc and/or QT interval of &gt; 60 ms were further evaluated for adverse reactions suggestive of drug-induced proarrhythmic events. The AE terms identified in E14 (Guidance for Industry, June 2017) supplemented by the following additional terms: arrhythmia, electrocardiogram QT prolonged, ventricular extrasystoles, ventricular pre-excitation, death NOS, unknown cause of death, and death unexplained were examined in this subgroup. A total of 5 patients among these 89 had one or more proarrhythmic events. In addition, 2 patients who had an adverse event of electrocardiogram QT prolonged, in the absence of a documented QTc and/or QT interval of &gt;500 ms or an increase from baseline in QTc and/or QT intervals of &gt; 60 ms, were also evaluated. There were no patients with torsade de pointes, ventricular tachycardia, ventricular fibrillation and flutter, or seizures. Four patients had an AE of electrocardiogram QT prolonged that was Grade 1 (N=3) or Grade 3 (N=1), 3 patients had ventricular extrasystoles (Grade 1), and one patient also had a Grade 1 nodal escape rhythm. Toripalimab was not withheld or terminated in any of the 7 patients. The one patient with a Grade 3 QT interval prolongation (QTcF 503 ms) was on flecainide (known to prolong QT interval) with a mean QTcF interval of 492 ms at baseline and had recently started cimetidine and tramadol (confounding causes of the increase in QTc). The patient was able to continue toripalimab.
L1555: ## *Toripalimab in Combination with Chemotherapy*
L1556: In Cohorts 5-8/POLARIS-02, among the 36 patients exposed to toripalimab in combination with a variety of platinum-based chemotherapy regimens who had a baseline and at least one post-baseline QT assessment recorded, the mean change from baseline for the QT interval was a decrease of 8.0 ms. No patients had a maximum post-treatment QT interval of &gt;500 ms, and 13 patients (21.7%) had a maximum increase in QT interval &gt;60 ms. The mean change from baseline for QTc interval was an increase of 6.6 ms, no patients had a maximum QTc interval of &gt; 500 ms, and 10 patients (16.7%) had a maximum increased in QTc of &gt; 60 ms.
L1557: (b) (4)
L1558: (toripalimab-tpzi)
L1559: Among the 18 patients with an increase from baseline in QTc and/or QT interval of &gt; 60 ms, two patients had adverse reactions suggestive of drug-induced proarrhythmic events. One patient (CT5-(b) (6)) had a Grade 3 SAE of syncope in the setting of bradycardia, hypotension, streptococcus infection/sepsis and hypomagnesemia. The other (CT5-(b) (6)) had Grade 1 ventricular extrasystoles. Toripalimab was not withheld for either patient and the AEs resolved.
L1560: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1561: Based on its product class (PD-1 blocking antibodies) and the demonstration that toripalimab does not induce cytokine release in unstimulated peripheral blood mononuclear cells, toripalimab is not expected to cause QT prolongation via the hERG channel. A thorough QT study was not performed as the risk of QT prolongation in patients treated with this monoclonal antibody is low. Exposure-QTc analyses were not performed.
L1562: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1563: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L1564: ## **Immunogenicity**
L1565: ## **Data:**
L1566: Anti-drug antibodies were assessed using an electrochemiluminescence assay. Two approaches were used to determine the cut-point for ADA positivity. The first used healthy volunteer sera and the second used pre-treatment patient sera. The first approach was employed at a central laboratory in China in CT1-4, CT6, CT7-1, CT7-2, and CT9, whereas the second approach was employed at central laboratories in China (CT5, CT8, CT12, CT14, and CT15) and the US (TAB001-01). There were no clinically important differences in ADA results according to method or site of the central laboratory (US vs China).
L1567: The incidence of ADA in all patients who received toripalimab monotherapy was 101/1066 (9.5%). The incidence of infusion-related reaction was 1.0% in those with ADA and 2.2% in those without ADA. The incidence of ADA among patients in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 who received toripalimab monotherapy was 7/190 (3.7%). In the PTAS population of Cohort 3/POLARIS-02, the ORR was 28.6% in those with ADA and 20.6% in those without ADA.
L1568: Among the 206 patients who received toripalimab in combination with platinum-based therapy, 8 (3.9%) developed ADA. The incidence of IRR was 1/8 (12.5%) in those with ADA and 10/198 (5.1%) in those without. When toripalimab was given in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine on JUPITER-02, ADA was detected in 5/146 (3.4%) patients. In the toripalimab arm of JUPITER-02, the ORR was 60.0% in those with ADA and 78.0% in those without.
L1569: (b) (4)
L1570: (toripalimab-tpzi)
L1571: In population PK analyses, ADA-positive status (both pre-existing and treatment-emergent; N=101) was correlated with a relative increase in clearance by 20%. See Module 2.7.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology for a discussion of the relationship between ADA and toripalimab pharmacokinetics.
L1572: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1573: The effect of ADA formation on efficacy could not be fully evaluated due to the small number of patients in Cohort 3/POLARIS-02 (n=7) and JUPITER-02 (n= 5) with treatment-emergent ADA. However, there was no suggestion of decreased effectiveness. Infusion-related reactions did not appear to be increased in patients with ADA.
L1574: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1575: Due to the low incidence of anti-drug antibodies, the effect of these antibodies on the pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, safety, or effectiveness of (b) (4) is unknown.

L1583: ## **8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability**
L1584: ## **Data:**
L1585: On JUPITER-02, the EORTC QLQ-C30 and the EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N35 were collected at baseline and prior to each cycle. Questionnaires were scored according to the EORTC scoring manual. If a subscale had more than 50% of the items completed, a pro-rated score was computed. Subscales with less than 50% of the items completed were considered missing. Summary statistics were calculated along with the mean change from baseline. CT15 Table 14.2.7.1 and Figure 15.2.7.1 summarize the EORTC QLQ-C30 global health status and each subscale. The scores for the global health status increased (worsened) very slightly during the treatment period in both arms. CT15 Table 14.2.7.2 and Figure 15.2.7.2 provide information on the results of the EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N35. No notable differences were apparent between the curves.
L1586: No clinical outcome assessments were performed on POLARIS-02.
L1587: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1588: TopAlliance does not intend to make any claims with respect to the patient reported outcomes.
L1589: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1590: FDA agrees with Applicant's position. FDA considers these analyses of patient-reported outcome (PRO) endpoints to be exploratory in nature.
L1591: ## **8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups**
L1592: ## **Data:**
L1593: ## **Age**
L1594: Analyses were conducted to assess the differential risk of toxicity in the elderly as compared with younger patients. POLARIS-02 recruited only 10 patients and JUPITER-02 only 14 patients age 65 or greater. This may be due to the relatively young median age of patients with NPC. Analyses of adverse events by age were limited to the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset. There were 237 patients age 65 or greater in the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset (n=1066). However, only 36 patients were \ge 75 years and adverse events were not assessed in this age group.
L1595: This table and all subsequent tables in Section 8.2.7 include the most common (\ge 10% of patients in the toripalimab monotherapy safety dataset in order of decreasing incidence in the safety dataset). Laboratory abnormalities that were reported as AEs are not included in these analyses.
L1596: The incidence of common AEs was similar in older and younger patients with the exception that patients \ge 65 years were more likely to report asthenia (31.6% vs. 22.1%) and decreased appetite (21.5% vs. 15.3%). Conversely, younger patients were more likely to experience musculoskeletal pain (21.4% vs. 13.1%) or fever (19.1% vs. 13.9%). No other AEs differed in incidence by \ge 5%.

L1606: ## Race and Region
L1607: Safety analyses were conducted by region rather than race. The majority of the patients were enrolled from China and only TAB001-01, conducted in the US, enrolled patients from a variety of races. Since the number of patients on TAB001-01, other than those who identified as Caucasian (N = 112) was small, AEs were analyzed by region and the results from TAB001-01 were compared to that of patients enrolled outside the US.
L1608: On TAB001-01, 121/139 patients received toripalimab 240 mg IV Q3W. Among the 139 patients, median age was 62 years, 52.5% were male, 80.6% were White, 6.5% Black or African-American, 5.8% Asian, and 7.2% Other. The median duration of exposure was 45 days. Among the 927 patients enrolled outside the US, median age was 55, 64.2% were male, and all were Asian. The median duration of exposure was 99 days. The majority of these 927 patients (851/927 [91.8%]) received toripalimab 3 mg/kg IV Q2W.
L1609: Asthenia, decreased appetite, constipation, and nausea were more commonly (\ge 5\%) reported by US patients while cough, hypothyroidism, weight decreased, and rash were more commonly (\ge 5\%) reported outside the US. There did not appear to be a clear pattern to these differences.
